\title{Statistic Preservation Is Not Security Preservation:\\Identification Limits of Outcome-Gated Screening\\for Federated-Learning Defense Composition}

\author{Anupam Mediratta\\
Orbis AI\\
\texttt{mediratta@gmail.com}}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
Layering federated-learning defenses risks a composition failure: upstream processing can destroy the discriminative property the downstream defense needs. The natural repair---require \textbf{preservation} of that property---is the wrong abstraction for security. \textbf{Our result is causal: downstream-statistic preservation is causally insufficient for attack-suppression preservation.} What follows is the experimental design that can actually test a composition mechanism; the screening rule is a \emph{consequence}, not the contribution.

Preservation is a \textbf{hierarchy}: the statistic's value~(P1), the ordering it induces~(P2), the resulting decision~(P3), the adversarial mass admitted~(P4), suppression itself~(P5). (P1)$\Rightarrow$(P2)$\Rightarrow$(P3) hold by definition; \textbf{below the decision the chain breaks in \emph{both} directions}, each break with a measured witness. Everything an invariance argument can certify---including the sufficient conditions we prove for one aggregator class---lives at (P1)--(P3) and \textbf{bounds no ASR}, while the security content sits at~(P4). Two \textbf{general} limits follow for any criterion stated at the statistic level. \textbf{Identification}: because candidates are gated on standalone effectiveness in the very metric the criterion predicts, effectiveness and mechanism co-vary across every certified composition, and no cross-defense contrast identifies the mechanism's contribution \emph{at any sample size}. \textbf{Coverage}: suppression emergent in a composition and absent from both constituents cannot satisfy an inherited-suppression gate, capping recall structurally.

Three empirical findings, by oracle intervention on two arms. With attenuation closed \emph{by construction}, Krum's \emph{decision} flips in up to $80\%$ of rounds while the adversarial mass it admits stays at exactly $0.000$ and ASR shows no evidence of the predicted increase, staying inside our pre-specified equivalence margin---pre-registered, and replicated on a second dataset and architecture. On the identical cell the effect \textbf{reverses sign} once adversarial influence is unpinned: the outcome-gated ladder \emph{falls} $0.272$ where the instrument \emph{rises} $0.098$. And heterogeneity closes the theory's own margin, RFA's all-round mean separation margin collapsing $3.24 \to 1.01$ at Dirichlet $\alpha{=}0.1$, where our bound certifies nothing. \textbf{The boundary results are general; our empirical claims---star-topology FL, CIFAR-10 but for the replicated arm---are not.} What survives is a conservative \textbf{evaluation-prioritization rule} for inherited robustness, not a validated predictor.
\end{abstract}

\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:intro}

Federated learning~\citep{mcmahan2017communication} enables collaborative model training without centralizing data, but its distributed nature introduces vulnerability to data poisoning~\citep{chen2017targeted,bagdasaryan2020backdoor}. The arms race between attacks and defenses~\citep{wang2020attack,xie2019dba,pillutla2022robust,sun2019can,bhagoji2019analyzing} has produced strong results on both sides, but these analyses share two assumptions: the adversary knows the exact defense employed, and has access to samples from the true data distribution. In practice the server's defense is an implementation detail that can be kept private, and the adversary's optimal play depends critically on which defense is active.

\noindent The natural repair for a composition failure---check that the upstream stage preserves the property the downstream defense reads---is the subject of this paper, and \textbf{our result is that the repair does not work, and that this is structural rather than a matter of choosing the property better}. \textbf{Stated sharply: even after controlling for standalone effectiveness and intervening \emph{directly} on the downstream statistic, changing that statistic can have little or no effect on suppression---and the effect reverses once the intervention is changed to hold adversarial influence fixed instead} ($-0.272$ where influence co-varies, $+0.098$ where it is pinned; §\ref{sec:targeted}). \emph{Security preservation} names the framing thesis's object throughout---that suppression of the committed attack survives composition, under the adversary model of §\ref{sec:prelim}. The two levels it decomposes into are kept distinct and never conflated: \emph{adversarial contribution} (the influence the adversary retains on the aggregate, (P4)) and \emph{attack-suppression preservation} (ASR below threshold, (P5)). None of the three ever means preservation of the downstream statistic, of the ordering it induces, or of the decision that follows. Statistic preservation is the abstraction any invariance argument about composition can certify; we formalize the levels at which it comes apart from security and build the interventions that separate them. \textbf{The second half of the contribution is the experimental design that can actually test a composition mechanism}, and it follows from the identification boundary below, which is what derives it---so we state the boundary before naming our own conditions.

\noindent\textbf{The general form of the boundary, stated before its FL instance}, because it is a property of outcome-gated screening rather than of our menu:
\begin{quote}
\emph{Any} screening criterion whose eligibility condition is defined on the \textbf{same outcome metric the criterion predicts} cannot be validated by cross-defense contrasts: the gate forces effectiveness and mechanism to co-vary across every certified candidate, so no choice of defenses off any menu breaks the confound. The admissible designs are \textbf{within-defense}.
\end{quote}

\noindent One scoping point up front, because it sharpens rather than weakens the claim: our C1 requires that some constituent already suppress the attack alone, so nothing here discovers defenses from nothing---it reasons about \emph{compositions of already-effective defenses}, the question a practitioner with a defense menu actually faces.

\noindent\fcolorbox{black}{black!5}{\parbox{0.965\linewidth}{\small
\textbf{The result, up front.} Preservation is a \emph{hierarchy}, not a property: the statistic's value~(P1), the ordering it induces~(P2), the decision that follows~(P3), the adversarial mass admitted~(P4), suppression itself~(P5). We show \ \fbox{(P1) $\Rightarrow$ (P2) $\Rightarrow$ (P3) \ $\not\Rightarrow$ \ (P4) \ $\not\Rightarrow$ \ (P5)}, the first two links holding \textbf{by definition}---equal values induce the same ordering, and a rule reading only that ordering cannot decide differently---and each of the last two broken by a \textbf{counterexample we measured}. \emph{(P3)$\,\not\Rightarrow$(P4):} Mode~S sweeps benign dispersion $\rho$ from $1$ to $54.6$ and flips Krum's decision in up to $80\%$ of rounds while the adversarial coefficient share stays pinned at exactly $0.266667$, the admitted adversarial mass at $0.000$, and ASR inside the pre-specified equivalence margin (Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}). \emph{(P4)$\,\not\Rightarrow$(P5):} \texttt{cos\_krum} holds statistic, ordering, decision and admission \emph{bit-identical} at every rung---$0/120$ selections changed---and its ASR falls $0.425$ across the ladder regardless, $0.173$ of it by the first rung, where the accuracy gate still passes. Hence, formally (Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability}, §\ref{sec:targeted}): \textbf{no level of this hierarchy is sufficient for security}---a non-existence result \emph{within} the positive-rescaling class on these defenses and attacks, exhibited by counterexample, and \textbf{not} a claim about every conceivable metric. \textbf{Everything an invariance argument can certify lives at (P1)--(P3); the security content is at (P4)}, and that gap---not the screening rule built over it---is this paper's subject. Definition~\ref{def:levels} states the levels formally and §\ref{sec:targeted} measures both breaks.
}}
\smallskip

The secondary result, and the scope of the experiments behind it, follow. The information asymmetry noted above raises a natural question of its own: does per-round aggregator switching provide security value? In centralized settings, randomized input transformations~\citep{xie2018mitigating} and random self-ensembles~\citep{liu2018towards} exploit attacker uncertainty, and production FL platforms~\citep{ludwig2020ibm} expose pluggable aggregators operators may rotate; but the backdoor-persistence literature~\citep{bagdasaryan2020backdoor,zhang2022neurotoxin} establishes that embedded triggers survive many rounds of partial suppression, which suggests persistence should eliminate switching value. We confirm and quantify that intuition---defense secrecy is cheaply probed (Appendix~\ref{app:inference}) and realized VoPD $\approx 0$ on standard menus---and the operational consequence, that deterministic composition outperforms switching on every tested menu, is what motivates the rest of the paper.

Moreover, defense choices are not free: each mechanism imposes accuracy and fairness costs~\citep{wang2020attack}, and attack strength depends on network heterogeneity, the defense in use, and model architecture. A game-theoretic formulation naturally captures these mutual dependencies.

\textbf{Scope.} We investigate whether \emph{per-round aggregator switching} (randomly selecting which defense processes updates each round) has positive value against persistent FL poisoning. Randomized internals within a single defense---DP noise, subsampling smoothing \`{a} la CRFL~\citep{xie2021crfl}, random participation masks---operate at a different level and are not addressed. Our experiments use a specific deployment regime (CIFAR-10/100, $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f \in \{0.05, 0.2\}$, 50--200 rounds, 4-layer CNN), within which we characterize a persistence boundary: above it (high $\gamma$, universally-admitting menus) randomization value collapses, below it (stateless attacks, orthogonal-suppression menus) it survives. Whether the boundary's location transfers to $N \geq 10^4$, $f \leq 0.01$ is open.

\textbf{Contributions.} Three, in order of what the paper is \emph{about}: (1) a preservation hierarchy and the measured break that makes statistic preservation the wrong security abstraction; (2) the identification boundary that break implies for any outcome-gated screen; (3) the causal interventions that locate where security is actually decided. \textbf{Three empirical findings carry (3), and we name them here because the rest of the paper is organized around them}: \emph{(i)} disturbing the statistic does not move suppression---instrumented, with the attenuation channel closed by construction, and replicated on a second dataset and architecture (§\ref{sec:targeted}); \emph{(ii)} a \textbf{sign reversal} between the outcome-gated and the instrumented design on the \emph{identical} cell ($-0.272$ against $+0.098$), which is what makes the identification boundary a measured hazard rather than a cautionary tale; \emph{(iii)} \textbf{heterogeneity closes the theorem's own margin}---RFA's all-round mean separation margin collapses $m{=}3.24 \to 1.01$ at Dirichlet $\alpha{=}0.1$ ($4.71 \to 2.06$ over the rounds with $w_a{>}0$), exactly where Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass} degrades to certifying nothing and two of three certified compositions cross the threshold. \textbf{The boundary results are general; our empirical claims are not.} Items (4) and (5) below are not further contributions: (4) is the screening rule, stated as a \emph{consequence} of (2), and (5) is one secondary result.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
    \item \textbf{The preservation hierarchy, and the two-directional break that makes statistic preservation the wrong abstraction.} Because the per-client defenses in use are all positive scalar rescalings, mechanism preservation is a decidable \emph{invariance} question (Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}), and on the weighting$\to$aggregator class we prove it with explicit margins ($\Delta_{\mathrm{signal}}$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{margin}}$; Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}), so that class needs no calibration runs. But preservation is not one property. It has \textbf{five levels}---the statistic's value, the ordering it induces, the decision that follows, the adversarial mass admitted, and suppression itself (Definition~\ref{def:levels})---and while (P1)$\Rightarrow$(P2)$\Rightarrow$(P3) hold by definition, \textbf{below (P3) the chain breaks in \emph{both} directions and we measure both breaks} (§\ref{sec:targeted}, §\ref{sec:conclusion}). \textbf{Everything an invariance argument can prove lives at (P1)--(P3); the security content is at (P4)}, and that gap, not the screen built over it, is this paper's subject. One corollary is worth stating alone: weight heterogeneity is safe at \emph{both} extremes and dangerous in the middle---with $S$ the attack's coordinate extremeness and $r$ the adversary's relative weight, $S\cdot r \gg 1$ leaves the adversary extreme enough to be discarded and $S\cdot r \ll 1$ annihilates its payload, while $S\cdot r \approx 1$ is the hazard---so \textbf{the weight ratio $\rho$ alone mispredicts} (Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}).

    \item \textbf{An identification boundary for outcome-gated screening, and a bound on what any such screen can find.} The general claim above is proved as Proposition~\ref{prop:identification}; its FL instance is that C0$\wedge$C1 \emph{force} the downstream defense of every certified in-scope pair to suppress the relevant attack on its own, so a certified composition's low ASR is always attributable to C1 as well as to C2---an effect we name \textbf{mechanism--effectiveness confounding}, structural rather than an artifact of our defense menu (§\ref{sec:testability}). It is not specific to FL. What we offer as the transferable contribution, though, is not this negative but \textbf{the admissible design it derives} (§\ref{sec:testability}). Its evidential cost is real: a matched metric-swap ablation built to break the confound had its premise refuted by its own pre-freeze baselines---magnitude information is what lets this defense family suppress a magnitude attack---so \textbf{the confound re-materialized inside an experiment built to break it} ($8/8$ frozen labels, TP $=0$; §\ref{sec:metric_swap}). A second boundary of the same shape limits coverage rather than validation: \textbf{emergent} suppression---present in the pair, absent in both constituents---cannot satisfy an inherited-suppression gate, so such a screen's recall is capped by the fraction of successes whose suppression is inherited (Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent}). That is a scope theorem rather than a defect found by tuning, and our own lowest-ASR composition is its witness (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}).

    \item \textbf{Causal interventions that locate the security channel---not a validation of any screen.} Three results, in the order the boundary licenses them, each tagged with its evidence tier. \emph{(i) Refuted---our own pooled statistic-level hypothesis} (\emph{Causal}). The boundary specifies a within-defense ladder holding C1 numerically fixed---same downstream defense, same attack, same standalone baseline---and varying only \emph{measured} disturbance of the statistic $d_2$ reads, over four arms spanning three invariance classes, four rungs $\rho = e^{2\kappa} \in \{1.00,2.72,7.39,54.60\}$ and five seeds, with the abscissa measured independently before the freeze. \textbf{We ran it ($80$ runs) and it mostly refutes us}: pooled $\rho_s = +0.351$, $p = 0.091$, two of four frozen shapes refuted, and ASR \emph{falling} as the dose rises in three arms of four, so we withdraw the pooled form of C2's causal claim (§\ref{sec:dose_response}). Reputation confirms ($0.017 \to 0.779$, $p = 1.7{\times}10^{-6}$, accuracy $0.78 \to 0.70$), covering the destruction half alone---and (ii) attributes most of that rise to attenuation rather than statistic disturbance. \emph{(ii) Adjudicated by intervention---statistic preservation is not security preservation} (\emph{Causal}, then \emph{Replication}). This is the paper's sharpest result and its title. An upstream pinning every adversary's coefficient (Mode~S: adversarial share constant at $0.267$) closes Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s attenuation channel \emph{by construction}: \textbf{Krum's decision flips in up to $80\%$ of rounds while the adversarial mass it admits stays at exactly $0.000$ and its ASR stays inside the pre-specified equivalence margin} (§\ref{sec:targeted}), so the implication from decision invariance to suppression preservation fails, and fails measurably. A \textbf{pre-registered replication} on \texttt{coord\_median}---a coordinate-wise order statistic rather than Krum's selector, chosen because it is \emph{rank-discordant} on the two predictors---\textbf{reproduces that negative on a different invariance class and refutes the positive ordering we had predicted} ($+0.098$, below an arm whose admission change was smaller), so \textbf{admission is the better direction but not a quantitative predictor} ($R^2{=}0.239$ over $26$ cells). \textbf{What this establishes, it establishes by oracle intervention on two arms in two of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s classes}---an \emph{identification} result on those arms, not a measured law about defense compositions in general; both instruments read adversary identity and are deliberately not deployable, and they rescore nothing in §\ref{sec:dose_outcome}. A companion payload-only sweep (Mode~A) confirms Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s single-peaked prediction for the rank aggregator it is stated over and refutes it off-spec, as the theorem predicts. \emph{(iii) Diagnosed---and the diagnosis refutes a third predictor.} $\rho$ says how far apart a transform spreads the coefficients; \textbf{adversary-correlated dispersion} $\Delta_c := \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ says \emph{who} receives it, and our formal results constrain $\rho$ alone. Measured for the four real transforms (post-hoc, no ASR), the two anchors that miss (i)'s synthetic curve are the two most extremely targeted ($\Delta_c = -1.21$ for RFA, $-0.72$ for NormClip, against $-0.34$ at the ladder's most dispersed rung) \textbf{at $\rho$ that is $3.7$ and $13\times$ smaller}. \textbf{But $\Delta_c$ does not order suppression either}: RFA targets the adversary hardest and its composition still fails. That makes three candidate predictors---statistic disturbance, admission magnitude, coefficient targeting---each refuted \emph{as a predictor}, which is why what survives is a preservation \emph{level} and not a statistic of the coefficient vector. Two propositions formalize what the instruments measured rather than adding a claim: \textbf{statistic-level non-identifiability} (Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability}), whose two witnesses are the arms above---so no metric defined \emph{solely} on preservation of the downstream statistic identifies security equivalence within this class---and the \textbf{independent controllability} of $\rho$ and $\Delta_c$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:rho_deltac}), which is why these two instruments, and not a single dial, were the right design.

    \item \textbf{The screening rule is a consequence of (2), not a claim of its own} (\emph{Screening} throughout). What survives the negative is a conservative \emph{evaluation-prioritization} rule: read C2 off the downstream signal's invariance class, then evaluate the survivors first. Used that way it is reliable in the positive direction and lossy: every composition the tightened criterion certifies does work (2 in-distribution, 4 out-of-sample), but \textbf{recall is only $40\%$} on a positive class of just $5/42$---the bound of Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent} in practice---and we do not offer the precision as validation, since it rests on a handful of positive predictions in a badly imbalanced evaluation (§\ref{sec:condition_scoring}). Scored across suites: $90.5\%$ on 42 two-way pairs (18 development \emph{fit}, $89\%$; 24 pre-registered held-out \emph{prediction}, $92\%$), $9/10$ pre-registered triples (§\ref{sec:three_way}), $13/20$ on a prospective suite of three previously unused defenses frozen in git before any run, and better than random selection ($p < 10^{-4}$). It certifies only \emph{inherited} robustness under a \emph{committed} attack and outputs an evaluation \emph{ordering}, never a deployment decision; among evaluated compositions it identifies an accuracy--security Pareto frontier: FG$\to$CM ($0.107$ / $0.68$) as the best candidate for further evaluation, FG$\to$RFA ($0.093$ / $0.50$) as the lowest-observed-ASR extreme, Rep$\to$CM ($0.423$ / $0.76$) for near-baseline accuracy (Table~\ref{tab:pareto}).

    \item \textbf{Secondary, and outside the three tiers---composition dominates mixing under persistence.} Deterministic composition outperforms temporal mixing (per-round defense switching) on every tested menu and adversary policy: oracle ASR $0.316$ under composition vs.\ $0.915$ under mixing on rep+tm; all compositions suppress to ${\approx}0.10$ at $N{=}100$/ResNet18 (§\ref{sec:framework}). The mechanism is backdoor persistence ($\gamma \approx 0.66$): once a trigger embeds, intermittent suppression cannot clear it, making mixes converge to their worst constituent's ASR. Committed strategies are near-optimal (largest measured switching gain $+0.044$, below bound $0.048$); only stateless threats ($\gamma{=}0$) permit positive randomization value (Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework}). Fixed-order composition also beats randomized ordering $9\times$, because preservation conditions are order-sensitive; and under \emph{one} whitebox composition-aware \emph{adaptive stress test} FG$\to$RFA's ASR rises to $0.167 \pm 0.069$, $3.3\times$ its seed-matched committed $0.051$, showing that \textbf{an inherited-robustness guarantee is not adaptive robustness} (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}).


\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textbf{Three tiers of evidence, of very unequal weight, never pooled.} Every empirical claim about the preservation hierarchy or the criterion is tagged with one of these three words at the point it is made, so a reader can look up what licenses it instead of reconstructing it from hedges. Contribution~5 carries no tier tag because it is outside them: the mixing comparison is motivation for studying composition at all, and no part of the hierarchy or the boundary rests on it (§\ref{sec:framework}).

\toprule

\midrule

\addlinespace
\textbf{Replication} & FEMNIST---second dataset \emph{and} architecture ($\Delta{=}-0.017$)---and \texttt{coord\_median}, a second invariance class ($+0.098$) & ``replicated on a second dataset and architecture'', \textbf{never} ``generalizes'' \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Screening} & the outcome-gated 42-pair suite, $38/42$ & \textbf{evaluation prioritization only}; by Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} it cannot identify the mechanism's contribution at \emph{any} sample size \\

\end{center}

\textbf{How the paper is organized, as an argument.} Composition creates an \textbf{identification problem}: the screening conditions themselves force effectiveness and mechanism to co-vary (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}). Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} \textbf{characterizes} that problem and, read as a derivation, \textbf{dictates the only admissible experiment} (§\ref{sec:testability}). We run that experiment, and it \textbf{falsifies our own statistic-level hypothesis} while adjudicating what replaces it (§\ref{sec:dose_response}, §\ref{sec:targeted}). What the invariance theory still \emph{explains}---the two mechanism-preserving regimes, and the margin collapse at the boundary between them---is §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem}. What survives as an \emph{application}, an ordering over an evaluation budget rather than a prediction, is §\ref{sec:condition_scoring}; §\ref{sec:conclusion} states the open problem the negative leaves.

\noindent\textbf{Why this class, and how to read every claim below.} Everything we \emph{prove} is instantiated for positive per-client rescalings, deliberately: the class is a \textbf{laboratory for studying composition, not a general model of it}---it covers the per-client defenses in current use (norm clipping, FoolsGold, RFA reweighting, FLTrust) and is clean enough to make invariance decidable from definitions. Defenses that alter participation, carry cross-round state, mix updates across clients, or aggregate before the next stage \textbf{fall outside it and are covered by nothing here}. \textbf{Every claim in this paper is one of exactly three kinds---\emph{general}, \emph{formal}, \emph{empirical}---and they do not transfer upward}: the formal guarantee does not certify the general framing, and the empirical figures certify neither level above them. Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels} states the three with every scope condition attached, and each claim below is labelled by kind at the point it is made; §\ref{sec:limitations} then gives the same conditions one at a time as (L1)--(L9).

\textbf{Reading guide.} The body presents the composition criterion (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}), its validation including 3-way compositions where exhaustive search is infeasible (§\ref{sec:composability_validation}, §\ref{sec:three_way}), the composition the screen cannot certify, FG$\to$RFA, characterized under a criterion-aware adversary as Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent}'s witness (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}), and a condensed case for why composition rather than mixing is the object of study (§\ref{sec:framework}); the full game-theoretic analysis is Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework}. The bridge between them is persistence: a composition applies both defenses every round, so a signal-preservation failure admits the attack on every round, and under high $\gamma$ those admissions accumulate and saturate ASR---converting a C2/C3 failure into total failure.

\section{Preliminaries}
\label{sec:prelim}

\textbf{Federated learning.} $N$ clients each hold a local dataset $\mathcal{D}_i \sim P_i$; the server learns $w^* = \arg\min_w \sum_i \frac{|\mathcal{D}_i|}{|\mathcal{D}|} F_i(w)$. In FedAvg~\citep{mcmahan2017communication}, each round the server broadcasts $w^t$, selected clients run local SGD, and the server aggregates $w^{t+1} = w^t + \frac{1}{|S_t|}\sum_{i \in S_t} \Delta_i^t$.

\textbf{Poisoning and robust aggregation.} An adversary controlling a fraction $f$ of clients can flip labels~\citep{chen2017targeted}, inject backdoor triggers~\citep{bagdasaryan2020backdoor,xie2019dba}, or scale malicious updates to dominate aggregation~\citep{bagdasaryan2020backdoor}. The server may replace FedAvg with a Byzantine-robust rule --- Krum~\citep{blanchard2017machine}, Trimmed Mean, Coordinate-wise Median, Norm Clipping~\citep{sun2019can}, or RFA (geometric median)~\citep{pillutla2022robust} --- each imposing an accuracy/robustness trade-off.

\textbf{Game theory.} A normal-form game is $G = (\mathcal{N}, \{\mathcal{S}_i\}, \{u_i\})$. A \emph{Bayesian game} adds a type space $\ThetaD$ and prior $\mu \in \Delta(\ThetaD)$; a profile is a \emph{Bayes-Nash Equilibrium (BNE)} if each player maximizes expected utility given beliefs over others' types.

\section{Composability Criterion}
\label{sec:composability_criterion}

The paper's central practical question is \emph{which} defense pairs compose well under deterministic joint application. Specifically, the criterion screens for \emph{mechanism-preserving composition}: when one constituent defense is already individually effective (C1), can the other defense be added without breaking that suppression? This is the operationally relevant question---the criterion is not a general characterization of all composable pairs, but a sufficient condition for \emph{mechanism preservation} among pairs where at least one component has known effectiveness---never a sufficient condition for low ASR. We answer it first, with a checkable criterion, and defer to §\ref{sec:framework} and Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework} the game-theoretic machinery (the persistence mechanism and complementarity diagnostic) that explains \emph{why} the alternative---temporal mixing---fails. \textbf{But the practical question is not what the paper establishes}: what it establishes is that preservation of the statistic the screen checks is not causally tied to the security the screen predicts, so the criterion below should be read as the application §\ref{sec:condition_scoring} scopes it to---an ordering over an evaluation budget---and not as a deployment recommendation.

Observe that each robust aggregation defense $d$ operates through an \emph{admission signal} $\sigma(u)$ applied to client update $u$: it accepts, clips, or down-weights $u$ based on whether $\sigma(u)$ exceeds a threshold. Crucially, the admission signal varies by defense family:
\begin{itemize}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Weighting defenses} (reputation, FoolsGold): $\sigma(u) = $ distance from consensus (L2) or pairwise similarity.
\item \textbf{Clipping defenses} (NormClip): $\sigma(u) = \|u\|_2$.
\item \textbf{Rank-based defenses} (TrimmedMean, CoordMedian): $\sigma_k(u) = $ coordinate-wise rank of $u_k$.
\item \textbf{Geometric defenses} (RFA): $\sigma(u) = $ distance from geometric median.
\end{itemize}
When two defenses are applied sequentially (d1 transforms the updates, d2 aggregates the result), d1's transformation may \emph{corrupt} d2's signal or vice versa.

\begin{criterion}[Signal-Preservation Composability]
\label{thm:composability}
Let $d_1$ and $d_2$ be defense mechanisms. The following sufficient conditions guarantee that composition $(d_1 \to d_2)$ suppresses attack $a$. They are of \textbf{two different kinds}, and we group them accordingly, because conflating an empirical screen with a mechanistic property is the main source of confusion about what the criterion claims.

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Stage 0 --- applicability.} Before either kind of condition is informative, the question the criterion asks has to be live.
\begin{description}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item[(C0) The downstream mechanism is load-bearing.] $\mathrm{ASR}(d_1,a) \geq 0.5$ for at least one committed attack $a$. C2 and C3 concern preservation of \emph{$d_2$'s} admission signal, so they say nothing when $d_1$ alone already suppresses everything: the composition inherits that suppression, destroying $d_2$'s signal costs nothing, and a correct prediction would be correct for a reason the criterion did not supply. The asymmetry is deliberate---a strong \emph{$d_2$} is the intended case, since asking whether upstream processing breaks an effective downstream defense is the framework's whole question, whereas a strong $d_1$ makes the question moot. C0 was \textbf{formulated post-hoc} and its provenance is in Remark~\ref{rem:c0}.
\end{description}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{An empirical effectiveness gate.} This screens; it does not explain, and it is the one condition that cannot be checked without running something.
\begin{description}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item[(C1) Individual suppression.] For \emph{every} committed attack $a$ in the evaluated set, $\min_{d\in\{d_1,d_2\}}\mathrm{ASR}(d,a) < 0.5$. The quantifier matters because our headline metric is the \emph{maximum} over attacks. C1 is an \textbf{empirical prerequisite rather than a mechanistic property}: it is defined through the same ASR threshold that constitutes the outcome. It also scopes the criterion, which sharpens rather than weakens it---the criterion does not discover defenses from nothing, it selects \emph{mechanism-preserving compositions of already-effective defenses}, which is the question a practitioner holding a defense menu actually faces.
\end{description}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Mechanism-preservation conditions.} These are what Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} proves for the weighting$\to$aggregator class, and they are determined by $d_1$'s and $d_2$'s definitions alone---no experiment required. \textbf{What we recommend is an \emph{evaluation-prioritization criterion}, C1$\wedge$C2---and we name it that way rather than a ``screen'' because that is the whole of what our evidence supports.} \textbf{We disclaim predictive validity explicitly, here at first use}: the criterion orders candidate compositions for evaluation, and it does \emph{not} predict whether a composition is secure. The pooled two-channel model over $26$ cells is unconfirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$), and our own pre-registered replication \textbf{refuted} the cross-arm admission ordering the criterion would need in order to predict (§\ref{sec:targeted}); recall is $40\%$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent}), so the negative direction carries no information either. \textbf{It is never a security screen, and a PASS is an evaluation ordering rather than a deployment decision} (Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}). C3 is retained as a \textbf{margin diagnostic} rather than a gate, because on our data it never binds on a pair C1$\wedge$C2 already admits and shows no incremental predictive value (§\ref{sec:condition_scoring}). \emph{The pre-registered labels of §\ref{sec:composability_validation} were frozen with C3 in the gate and are not relabelled}; the demotion is a recommendation going forward, supported by a post-hoc factorial ablation, and it changes no reported number---C1$\wedge$C2 selects exactly the same two in-distribution pairs.
\begin{description}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item[(C2) Signal preservation.] $d_1$'s per-client transformation $T_1$ does not destroy $d_2$'s admission signal: $\sigma_2(T_1(u_a)) \approx \sigma_2(u_a)$ for malicious updates $u_a$.
\item[(C3) Boundary preservation.] $d_1$'s transformation does not compress the adversarial--benign separation below $d_2$'s discrimination threshold. Formally: if $d_2$ suppresses $a$ by detecting $\tau_{d_2}(u_a) > \theta$ (triggering trimming/rejection), then $\tau_{d_2}(T_1(u_a)) > \theta$ must hold---i.e., $d_1$ must not map adversarial updates from above to below $d_2$'s rejection boundary.
\end{description}
When C1 holds but C2 fails (signal destruction), composition is no better than $d_2$ alone. When C1 and C2 hold but C3 fails (boundary compression), $d_2$ retains its admission signal direction but its threshold no longer fires---the attack passes because $d_1$'s rescaling places adversarial magnitudes within $d_2$'s acceptance region.
\end{criterion}

Writing C2 as preservation of $d_2$'s admission signal elides a distinction our own experiments later force, so we name the levels before relying on the condition. They are the paper's main conceptual instrument, and every claim below is stated at one of them.

\begin{definition}[Preservation levels]
\label{def:levels}
For an upstream transform $T_1$ and a downstream defense $d_2$ reading admission signal $\sigma_2$:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt,label=(P\arabic*)]
\item \textbf{Value invariance:} $\sigma_2(T_1(U)) = \sigma_2(U)$.
\item \textbf{Ordering invariance:} $\sigma_2(T_1(U)) = \varphi(\sigma_2(U))$ for some $\varphi$ preserving the ordering $\sigma_2$ induces on clients. \emph{This is the level C2 is stated at} (made explicit in \eqref{eq:general_form}); the distinction from (P1) matters, because exact values are sufficient but never necessary.
\item \textbf{Decision invariance:} the client or coordinate $d_2$ actually selects is unchanged.
\item \textbf{Adversarial contribution (admission) invariance:} the \emph{adversarial} input $d_2$ admits into its aggregate is unchanged. This is a \textbf{level, not one measurable quantity}: for a selector it is the indicator that an adversarial update is selected at all; for a weighted averager, the adversarial share of the weight mass; for a coordinate-wise order statistic, the adversarial share of selected coordinates---which is why Table~\ref{tab:channels}'s influence column is aggregator-specific by necessity. The committed pre-registrations say ``admission'', so that word is retained in every frozen rule name and no scored quantity is relabelled.
\item \textbf{Attack-suppression preservation:} ASR stays below threshold.
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{(P1)$\Rightarrow$(P2)$\Rightarrow$(P3) hold, and hold by definition}---equal values induce the same ordering, and a rule reading only that ordering cannot decide differently. \textbf{These three are logical implications under the definitions above, not empirical claims about how defenses behave}, and nothing is asserted by proving them: their content is that they stop at (P3). \textbf{Below (P3) the chain breaks, and it breaks in both directions.} Both breaks are measured. \emph{Holding} decision \emph{and} admission invariance does not deliver suppression: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every dose and its admitted adversarial mass is $0.000$ throughout, yet its ASR falls $0.173$ by the first rung alone through the magnitude channel---at $\rho{=}2.72$, where clean accuracy moves only $0.599 \to 0.567$ and our accuracy gate passes (paired $t{=}{-}1.976$, one-sided $p{=}0.044$, falling in $5/8$ seeds). Across the whole ladder it falls $0.425$, but that endpoint sits below our own accuracy floor, so \textbf{the gate-passing rung is the witness we rest on} (§\ref{sec:targeted}). \emph{Losing} decision invariance does not cost suppression: Krum's selection flips in up to $80\%$ of rounds under an intervention that pins the adversarial mass, and its ASR stays inside the pre-specified equivalence margin ($|\Delta|{=}0.026 < 0.15$) (§\ref{sec:targeted}). A criterion stated at (P1)--(P2) therefore constrains security in \emph{neither} direction. \textbf{(P4) is where the security content sits---but we do not claim it is sufficient for (P5).} It is the first level that refers to \emph{adversarial influence on the aggregate} rather than to the downstream defense's internal decision, and \texttt{cos\_krum} is our own witness that (P4)$\;\not\Rightarrow\;$(P5): admission identically $0.000$ at every rung while suppression moves. \textbf{So the hierarchy answers ``which level does a security claim have to be stated at?'' and not ``which level predicts ASR?''}---no level in it is offered as a predictor.
\end{definition}

\noindent \textbf{The full causal chain, and the links we actually measure.} Definition~\ref{def:levels} is a prefix of a longer chain: statistic $\to$ \emph{decision} $\to$ \emph{admission} $\to$ \emph{aggregate adversarial influence} $\to$ per-round model update $\to$ attack persistence $\to$ \emph{ASR}. We measure the four emphasized links---decision, admission, aggregate influence ($\Lambda_a$) and ASR---and we do \textbf{not} measure per-round update geometry or persistence dynamics, so every claim below is a claim about the measured links only, with the unmeasured two left as an acknowledged gap rather than assumed benign (Figure~\ref{fig:story}).

\begin{remark}[Which level C2 asserts, and which one is load-bearing]
\label{rem:levels}
At $\rho = 54.6$ the dose ladder of §\ref{sec:dose_response} changes Krum's \emph{decision} in $86.7\%$ of rounds while changing its \emph{admission} of adversarial input in $13.3\%$, and Krum's suppression does not deteriorate at all. A selection that flips from one benign client to another disturbs the statistic maximally and the mechanism not at all. \textbf{Terminology, once and for all: ``\emph{mechanism}'' always means the downstream defense's \emph{discriminative operation}---what separates adversarial from benign updates---and never its security behaviour, since that implication is precisely what this paper refutes.} We reserve ``mechanism preservation'' for the formal property Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} establishes, saying \emph{statistic} or \emph{decision} preservation where only the weaker property holds. C2 above is the (P2) condition---which is what makes it checkable from definitions alone---but the load-bearing reading is (P4). \textbf{This is the paper's central tension: our theorem operates at (P1)--(P3), while the experiments indicate security behaviour is decided at (P4)}; §\ref{sec:targeted} puts the two against each other in an experiment where they predict opposite outcomes, and Figure~\ref{fig:story} is that argument in one picture.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[C0's provenance: an applicability condition formulated post-hoc]
\label{rem:c0}
C0 sits in Criterion~\ref{thm:composability} as a stage-0 applicability condition because Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} is stated over C0$\wedge$C1, but \textbf{it does not have the same evidential status as C1--C3}, and we record that here rather than let its promotion obscure it. It is \textbf{vacuous on the 7-defense menu of §\ref{sec:composability_validation}}, where the strongest single defense reaches only $0.519$, and we formulated it only after the prospective suite of §\ref{sec:prospective} introduced a defense for which it binds. It is a lesson from prospective evaluation, applied there retrospectively; it is \emph{not} part of the criterion validated on the 42 pairs, and none of the accuracy figures in §\ref{sec:composability_validation} depend on it.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{A scope condition on the criterion itself, not a limitation of our runs.} C1 and every prediction built on it are defined against a \emph{committed} adversary---one whose attack is fixed before the composition is chosen. An adversary who observes the composition can re-target whichever channel the screen certifies, so \textbf{what the screen bounds is \emph{inherited} robustness under a fixed attack, never robustness against an adaptive one}. Our single whitebox composition-aware stress test is consistent with that reading ($0.051 \to 0.167$ seed-matched, $3.3\times$ the committed-attack ASR; §\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}); \textbf{its role is not to measure adaptive robustness but to show that an inherited-robustness guarantee is not adaptive robustness}, and a screening condition stated against an adaptive adversary is left open.

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering\small
\caption{\textbf{Scope of claims: three kinds, and the one place each scope condition is stated.} \textbf{Claims do not transfer upward}---the formal guarantee does not certify the general framing, and the empirical figures certify neither level above them. This table is the conservation record for every caveat in the paper; §\ref{sec:limitations} restates the same conditions individually as (L1)--(L9).}
\label{tab:scope_levels}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.115\linewidth}p{0.315\linewidth}p{0.505\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{kind} & \textbf{content} & \textbf{status, and what it does \emph{not} license} \\
\midrule
\textbf{General} & An evaluation gated on the outcome it predicts cannot identify the mechanism's contribution, and admissible designs are within-defense; an inherited-suppression gate cannot certify emergent synergy (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}, Prop.~\ref{prop:emergent}). & \textbf{Proved, and not specific} to FL, to backdoors, or to our conditions. Licenses no claim that any composition \emph{is} secure, and none that mechanism preservation implies suppression. Recall is bounded, not achieved: $40\%$ here, from $|\mathcal{E}|/|\mathcal{S}|{=}1/5$ (L5, L7). \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Formal} & Mechanism preservation as \emph{invariance} under \textbf{positive per-client rescalings} $T(u_i)=c_iu_i$, $c_i>0$, into specific rank and geometric aggregators, with explicit margins (Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}, Lem.~\ref{lem:annihilation}, Cor.~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}, Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}, Prop.~\ref{prop:rank_invariance}). & \textbf{Proved on that class only}---a \emph{laboratory} for composition, not a model of FL defenses in general. A $d_1$ that alters participation, carries cross-round state, mixes clients, or aggregates first admits no such $T$ and is \textbf{covered by nothing here}. \textbf{Bounds no ASR.} C0 was formulated post-hoc and applied retrospectively to arms frozen before it (Rem.~\ref{rem:c0}, L7). Regime~A's condition is not operationally checkable, $S$ not being a stable per-attack constant (L8). Cor.~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound goes vacuous as $m \to 1$, which heterogeneity realizes. \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Empirical} & §\ref{sec:targeted}'s interventions and §\ref{sec:composability_validation}--§\ref{sec:prospective}'s 42-pair menu: CIFAR-10, CifarCNN/ResNet18, $N \in \{10,100\}$, $K \in \{5,10\}$, $f \in [0.05,0.6]$, Dirichlet $\alpha \in \{0.1,0.5\}$, \textbf{star topology}, three attack families; one arm on FEMNIST/\texttt{simple\_cnn}. & \textbf{Measured} at $n{=}5$ seeds per cell ($n{=}8$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}, $n{=}3$ on FEMNIST, $n{=}30$ for the FG$\to$RFA characterization), all fixed before the runs, so intervals are wide and \textbf{no null is read as flatness} without a pre-registered equivalence margin. ASR is thresholded at $0.5$, the level at which the trigger fires on the majority of triggered inputs; a cell below the $0.35$ clean-accuracy floor is \emph{uninterpretable}, not suppressed. Dataset transfer is tested on the flagship arm alone---``replicated on a second dataset and architecture'', \textbf{never} ``generalizes'' (L3). \textbf{``Refuted''} always means a pre-registered \emph{directional} hypothesis was rejected against a rule frozen for it, \textbf{never} that a mathematical claim was disproved. The $\gamma{\approx}0.66$ boundary is architecture-conditional (L2, L3, L6); the strongest development-set figures are \emph{fit}, not prediction (L4). \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Both instruments are oracles} & Mode~S and Mode~A build $c_i$ from adversary identity, as does $\Delta_c$ (§\ref{sec:targeted}, §\ref{sec:rho_vs_deltac}). & They \textbf{identify a causal channel and are not screens}: no deployed defense knows which clients are adversarial, and estimating (P4) without that knowledge is the open problem of §\ref{sec:conclusion}. Admission is \emph{a direction to measure}, not a validated predictor---the pooled two-channel model is unconfirmed over $26$ cells ($R^2{=}0.239$) and its cross-arm ordering was refuted by our own replication (L9). C1 and everything built on it assume a \textbf{committed} adversary, so what is bounded is \emph{inherited} robustness under a fixed attack, \textbf{never adaptive robustness}. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{The testability boundary}
\label{sec:testability}

C0 and C1 together constrain what any validation of this criterion can look like. We did not anticipate this; it turns out to explain the central weakness of our own prospective suite (§\ref{sec:prospective}, L7), and---once stated in general form---to \emph{derive} the class of within-defense designs in which C2's contribution is identifiable (§\ref{sec:dose_response}). Its content is narrower than ``a general identification boundary'' and worth stating precisely: \textbf{under inherited-effect gating}, cross-defense contrasts cannot identify the mechanism's contribution, because the gate conditions on a descendant of both the mechanism and the outcome. What we offer as the transferable contribution is not that negative but \textbf{the admissible design it derives}, set out below and run in §\ref{sec:targeted}.

\begin{lemma}[Testability boundary]
\label{lem:testability}
Let $(d_1 \to d_2)$ satisfy C0 and C1, and let $a^*$ be a committed attack witnessing C0, so $\mathrm{ASR}(d_1,a^*) \geq 0.5$. Then $\mathrm{ASR}(d_2,a^*) < 0.5$: the downstream defense suppresses $a^*$ \emph{on its own}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
C1 at $a^*$ gives $\min_{d\in\{d_1,d_2\}}\mathrm{ASR}(d,a^*) < 0.5$. By C0 the minimum is not attained at $d_1$, hence it is attained at $d_2$.
\end{proof}

Nothing in the argument is specific to federated learning, to backdoors, or to our defense menu. Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} is \textbf{the enabling observation for the FL instance}; the substantive claim is the general one, and we state it as such.

\begin{proposition}[Why constituent-effect contrasts cannot identify composition effects under inherited-effect gating]
\label{prop:identification}
Let a screen certify $(d_1 \to d_2)$ against attack $a$ only if both
(i) an \textbf{eligibility gate} $E(d_1,d_2,a) := \min_{d \in \{d_1,d_2\}} M(d,a) < \theta$ holds, where $M$ and $\theta$ are the \emph{same} outcome metric and threshold the screen predicts for the composition, and
(ii) a \textbf{mechanism condition} $P(d_1,d_2)$ holds, determined by the two defenses' definitions alone.
Suppose the screen is applied only where the downstream mechanism is load-bearing, $M(d_1,a) \ge \theta$. Consider estimating $P$'s contribution to $M(d_1 \to d_2, a)$ from contrasts across a defense menu $\mathcal{D}$. Then every certified pair satisfies $M(d_2,a) < \theta$, so of the four cells of $\{P,\neg P\} \times \{M(d_2,a) < \theta,\ M(d_2,a) \ge \theta\}$ the certified set occupies at most two, and \textbf{the cell needed to separate $P$ from $d_2$'s standalone effectiveness is empty by the gate, for every $\mathcal{D}$}. No contrast varying $d_2$ identifies $P$'s contribution. Identification requires holding $d_2$, $a$ and therefore $E$ fixed while intervening on the upstream transform: \textbf{the admissible designs are within-defense.}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The load-bearing condition excludes $d_1$ from attaining the minimum in (i), so certification forces $M(d_2,a) < \theta$; both cells with $M(d_2,a) \ge \theta$ are therefore unpopulated whatever $\mathcal{D}$ is, and $P$ is never observed against a downstream defense that fails on its own. Any estimator of $P$'s contribution from such contrasts is unidentified.
\end{proof}

\noindent \textbf{The structure is a collider, and the design table is the whole argument.} The gate $E$ is a function of constituent effectiveness; effectiveness affects the composition's outcome; and the mechanism condition $P$ is fixed by the same pair of definitions---so conditioning on $E$ makes $P$ and effectiveness co-vary across every certified candidate. Enumerating the four cells makes the missing counterfactual visible as \emph{a cell, not a sample size}:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule
& $M(d_2,a) < \theta$ \emph{($d_2$ suppresses $a$ alone)} & $M(d_2,a) \ge \theta$ \emph{(it does not)} \\
\midrule
$P$ holds (mechanism preserved) & observed: every certified pair & \textbf{empty by the gate} \\
$\neg P$ (mechanism destroyed) & observed: predicted-FAIL pairs & \textbf{empty by the gate} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Reading down a column contrasts $P$ against $\neg P$ at fixed effectiveness, which is the comparison that would identify $P$'s contribution---and the right-hand column, the only one in which $d_2$ is not already doing the work, is empty for \emph{every} menu $\mathcal{D}$. \textbf{The problem is not insufficient sample size: the counterfactual needed to identify the mechanism's contribution is excluded by design.} The emptiness follows from the gate, not from the menu.

\noindent In our instance $M$ is max-committed ASR, $\theta = 0.5$, $E$ is C1, the load-bearing condition is C0 and $P$ is C2$\wedge$C3---which is Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} read as identification rather than as arithmetic. The proposition is about the \emph{shape} of the screen, so it holds for any eligibility gate defined on the predicted outcome, in any domain. \textbf{This is a \emph{general} claim in the sense of Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}}: nothing in it is specific to FL, to backdoors, or to our conditions.

\paragraph{The payoff is the admissible design, not the proof.} These proofs are short, and their value is not difficulty but \textbf{what they rule in}: because eligibility is defined on the very outcome being predicted, effectiveness and mechanism co-vary across every certified candidate, so \emph{no} cross-defense contrast can identify the mechanism's contribution, and the only admissible instrument is one that holds $d_2$, the attack, and therefore the gate's input fixed while intervening on the upstream. That single sentence is what licenses every experiment in §\ref{sec:dose_response}--\ref{sec:targeted}: each is a member of the derived class, chosen because Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} said to look there rather than at another defense menu. A result can be easy to prove and still determine what one runs next.

\paragraph{This is selection on the outcome, and saying so is the point.} Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} is an instance of a hazard that is entirely standard outside this literature: conditioning on a variable that is a descendant of both the putative cause and the outcome---\textbf{selection on the dependent variable}, or collider conditioning \citep{elwert2014endogenous,pearl2009causality}. \textbf{We claim no novelty for the hazard.} What is not standard is \emph{where it hides}. In composition studies the gate is not presented as conditioning at all but as a \emph{reasonableness} filter---``we study defenses that work''---so it reads as good hygiene rather than as the step that removes identification. Three consequences are worth stating flatly, because each is routinely assumed away. \textbf{(i) More data cannot fix it}: the separating cell is empty by the gate, not by sampling, so it stays empty as the menu, the attack count and the seed count all grow---the estimator is not noisy, it is unidentified. \textbf{(ii) Nor can a better estimator}, since no reweighting of occupied cells recovers an unoccupied one. \textbf{(iii) Nor can the gate simply be dropped}: without it C1 is vacuous and the screen certifies compositions in which nothing suppresses the attack at all. The boundary is therefore not a limitation of our menu but a property of the design.

\paragraph{Both worked instances are ours, and one of them inverted a sign.} We name no other paper as making this mistake; we do not have to, because our own two designs are the instances. The C1-gated composition study of §\ref{sec:composability_validation} is the template at the level of the \emph{criterion}---which is exactly why Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} exists---and the dose ladder of §\ref{sec:dose_response} is a second instance one level down, where the gated quantity is not C1 but the dose's incidental attenuation of whichever client happens to carry the poison. \textbf{We ran the outcome-gated design first, and on the identical cell it returned the opposite sign}: the confounded ladder \emph{falls} $0.272$ ($p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0015$) where the instrument that pins adversarial influence \emph{rises} $+0.098$ (§\ref{sec:targeted}). Table~\ref{tab:comparability} puts the two designs side by side so the contrast can be checked rather than taken on trust: they share their $\kappa{=}0$ cell numerically and are matched at the top rung on aggregate displacement, decision change and clean accuracy. We can offer no stronger evidence that the hazard is not hypothetical: it cost us the sign of our own effect, in the direction that flattered us.


\begin{table}[t]
\centering\small
\caption{\textbf{The confounded ladder against the Mode-S instrument, on the one arm that has both.} Same downstream defense, same attack, same seeds, same nominal dose---and the two designs share their $\kappa{=}0$ cell \emph{numerically} (mean and worst per-seed gap $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$, asserted at $10^{-12}$). At the top rung they are matched on aggregate displacement, decision change and mean clean accuracy, so \textbf{the ladder's fall is not an accuracy collapse}; they differ chiefly in the \textbf{adversarial coefficient channel}, and the ASR effect \textbf{changes sign}. \textbf{The one non-match, disclosed:} aggregate adversarial influence $\Lambda_a$ moves in \emph{both} designs, $0.081$ against $0.033$ ($2.4\times$), so neither column is a clean statistic-only intervention---which is why the contrast is ``differ chiefly in the coefficient channel'', never ``only in it''. \emph{Vocabulary:} this row is the $\Delta\Lambda_a$ column of the channel table---the adversarial share of selected coordinates, the same field in the same artifact---and \textbf{not} its $\Delta$~adm.\ column, which counts rounds where the \emph{support} of the adversarial mass changes and reads $0.000$ on every row: what moves here is how much weight the adversary's direction carries, not whether it enters at all. This is a two-column contrast on one arm: it locates where the two designs differ, and attaches no $p$-value to the difference between the columns. Every cell recomputed from per-seed and per-round rows by \texttt{build\_comparability\_table.py}; nothing here is retyped.}

\midrule
