Does admitted mass \emph{order} outcomes across defenses? & pre-registered & \textbf{No---refuted}: $+0.098$ against a frozen ${+}0.150$ \\
Is (P4) \emph{sufficient} for (P5)? & control arm & \textbf{No}: \texttt{cos\_krum} admits $0.000$ while ASR falls \\
Can (P4) be screened \emph{without} adversary identity? & \textbf{no---open} & Both instruments are oracles: the stated open problem \\
Does C1 identify C2's contribution? & \textbf{no---structural} & Empty cell by the gate, for every menu (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}) \\
Does any of this certify \emph{deployed} security? & \textbf{no---scope} & Committed attack only; one adaptive test breaks it \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\textbf{What follows is a consequence of the identification boundary, not a claim the paper argues for.} Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} says a criterion gated on the outcome it predicts cannot be validated by cross-defense contrast; what such a criterion can still do is \emph{order an evaluation budget}, and that is the only use we claim for it. Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent} bounds what it can find. Read the numbers below as a measurement of a prioritization heuristic's cost, not as validation of the framework.

\textbf{Does every condition earn its place? C3 does not---reported as a finding.} Scoring all 42 pairs on C1, C2 and C3 \emph{independently} rather than recording only the first failure: against a $12\%$ base rate of low-ASR pairs, C1 alone selects 4 at $50\%$ precision, C2 alone 14 at $36\%$, and C1$\wedge$C2 selects 2 at $100\%$. \textbf{C3 has no demonstrated incremental predictive value in this study}---alone it selects 35 pairs at $9\%$ precision, below the base rate, and it never binds on a pair C1$\wedge$C2 already admits. So \textbf{we demote C3 from a gate to a margin diagnostic and recommend C1$\wedge$C2 as the criterion}, retaining C3 on theoretical grounds only: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} shows margin compression is a real failure mode, and the $\alpha{=}0.1$ regime below is precisely a margin collapse. \textbf{The demotion changes no number}---C1$\wedge$C2 selects the same two pairs at the same precision (recomputed by \texttt{analyze\_screening\_cost.py})---which is why we report it as a finding about the criterion rather than a revision of results. \emph{The pre-registered PASS/FAIL labels and the $16/18$ and $22/24$ tallies were frozen with C3 in the gate and are not relabelled}; this ablation is post-hoc and labelled as such, and the recommendation is for use going forward.

\textbf{C2 vs.\ C3 failure modes.} The distinction remains operationally meaningful: the C2-FAIL pairs land at 0.798--0.882 (signal destroyed), while C3-FAIL pairs range over 0.43--0.85 (partial suppression often remains). C2 failures are irrecoverable; C3 failures admit rescue via threshold adjustment or a rank-robust $d_2$. After the Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} reclassification the C2 category is also more coherent than before: \emph{every} surviving C2 failure is a $\cdot\to$reputation pair (nc$\to$rep $0.819$, rfa$\to$rep $0.882$, fg$\to$rep $0.798$), which is exactly the non-invariant signal class, while all three non-degenerate $\cdot\to$FoolsGold pairs are C1 failures. The taxonomy tightened rather than loosened when the error was corrected.

\textbf{Order sensitivity.} Composition is order-sensitive: the criterion predicts different failure modes for $(d_1 \to d_2)$ vs.\ $(d_2 \to d_1)$, and empirical outcomes confirm asymmetry. Two examples from our data:
\begin{itemize}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{rep$\to$NC} (max ASR $0.813$, C1-FAIL): reputation cannot suppress pixel (baseline $0.842$); NC adds nothing for pixel since it only clips norms. vs.\ \textbf{NC$\to$rep} (max ASR $0.819$, C2-FAIL): NC equalizes all norms, destroying reputation's consensus-distance signal. Both fail, but through mechanistically distinct pathways predicted by the criterion.
\item \textbf{fg$\to$rfa} (max ASR $0.093$ at $n{=}30$, PASS): FG downweights adversarial clients while preserving RFA's geometric-median signal. The reverse, \textbf{rfa$\to$fg} (max ASR $0.914$), fails for a different reason than one might expect: RFA-reweighting is a positive per-client rescaling, so by Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) it leaves FoolsGold's similarities \emph{exactly} intact and C2 holds. The pair fails C1---neither RFA alone ($0.885$) nor FoolsGold alone ($0.732$) suppresses. Order still matters, but here because of what each defense can do rather than what the upstream transform destroys.
\end{itemize}
The criterion's C2/C3 conditions are inherently asymmetric: they ask whether $d_1$'s output preserves $d_2$'s admission signal, not vice versa. The asymmetry is deliberate, and it is what lets the two orderings of a pair be \emph{ranked for evaluation} before either is run. Which ordering is in fact better is settled by evaluating it; the ranking predicts nothing about that.

\textbf{Continuous metric analysis.} To test whether the binary PASS/FAIL threshold is fragile we compute threshold sensitivity and rank correlation across all \textbf{42} matched pairs (each pair's max-committed ASR against its pre-registered category). \emph{Threshold sensitivity} is scored against the \emph{pre-registered} labels, so the two speculative-LOW held-out misses count as misses rather than being re-coded: accuracy is $92.9\%$ at $0.3$, $95.2\%$ at $0.4$, $\mathbf{90.5\%}$ at $0.5$ (identical to the headline $38/42$), $85.7\%$ at $0.6$ and $83.3\%$ at $0.7$---the $0.5$ operating point is not cherry-picked, varying by under $5$ points across $[0.3,0.5]$. \emph{Rank correlation} on the mechanism ordinals (PASS$=$1, C3-FAIL$=$2, C2-FAIL$=$3, C1-FAIL$=$4, DEGEN$=$5) gives Spearman $\rho = 0.38$ ($p = 0.014$, $n = 42$), but \emph{the signal is PASS-vs-rest, not a graded severity ranking}: PASS pairs (mean ASR $0.176 \pm 0.129$) separate cleanly from every failure mode (rest mean $0.787$, range $[0.43,0.94]$), while the taxonomy does \emph{not} order magnitude (C3-FAIL $0.66$, C2-FAIL $0.86$, C1-FAIL $0.81$, DEGEN $0.81$: non-monotone in the ordinal). We therefore claim only the effective-versus-ineffective cut, which is the practitioner-relevant one. (Correlations pool ASR at $n{=}3$--$30$ seeds; the PASS-vs-rest gap dwarfs that heteroscedastic noise.)

\textbf{Blind composition-selection comparison} (\emph{Screening}). This compares the criterion's ordering against uniform sampling \emph{on this menu}, and it is outcome-gated like the rest of the tier: by Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} it cannot establish that the conditions are what did the ordering. Against a blind baseline (uniformly sample $k{=}3$ pairs from all $42$), criterion-guided selection (the 3 PASS pairs, mean ASR $0.176$) beats $\mathbf{99.99\%}$ of random selections---only $0.01\%$ of uniformly sampled 3-pair subsets achieved comparable or lower mean ASR (finite-population resampling test, $10^5$ draws; $p < 10^{-4}$). Random selection yields mean ASR $0.744$ (95\% CI $[0.465, 0.898]$); only $0.1\%$ of random selections have all $k$ pairs below ASR $0.5$. At the 3-way level (10 measured of 210 possible triples): the 6 criterion-selected triples (mean ASR $0.078$) beat $99.7\%$ of random 6-of-10 draws ($p = 0.003$). On this menu, then, the ordering is worth more than uniform sampling for \emph{spending an evaluation budget}---which is the only use claimed. It does not identify the best compositions: recall is $40\%$, and the lowest-ASR composition in the paper lies outside the admitted set by construction. Nor is the comparison a validation, for the reason stated at the head of this subsection.

\textbf{What the saving is, counted in runs rather than pairs.} A pair count overstates it, because C1 is itself an empirical gate and its standalone evaluations are runs too---so the rule eliminates nothing ``before any run,'' and we previously described it that way in error. What it eliminates is the quadratic term. Counted per run (\texttt{analyze\_screening\_cost.py}; $7$ defenses, $42$ ordered pairs, $2$ committed attacks, $5$ seeds per cell): evaluating every ordered pair costs \textbf{$420$ runs}, while screening costs \textbf{$80$}---$60$ standalone (the C1 input, at $(D{-}1)\times|A|\times S$, since fedavg-as-upstream \emph{is} the standalone protocol) plus $20$ on the two admitted pairs. That is \textbf{$340$ runs, or $81\%$ fewer composition-evaluation runs}, not the $95.2\%$ of \emph{pairs} we previously reported.\footnote{The counterfactual above fixes the budget at 5 seeds per cell for comparability. What we actually spent on composition cells is $356$ runs over all $84$ cells, because seed counts vary by tier: 14 screened-out development pairs at 3 seeds, 2 at 5, all 24 held-out pairs at 5, and the 2 admitted pairs at 3, giving $14{\times}2{\times}3 + 2{\times}2{\times}5 + 24{\times}2{\times}5 + 2{\times}2{\times}3 = 356$. We state the mix rather than quoting a round number.} The structural claim is about scaling: from $|A|\,S\,D(D{-}1)$, \emph{quadratic} in the defense count, to $|A|\,S\,((D{-}1) + |\text{admitted}|)$, linear plus the admitted set. Two caveats belong with the numbers. \textbf{No per-run timing telemetry was ever recorded}, so the corresponding ${\approx}77 \to {\approx}15$ GPU-hour figures are \emph{estimates} at ${\approx}11$ min per 50-round run on the hardware described above; and the standalone baselines have independent value---they are the single-defense results we report anyway---so charging them wholly to screening is the conservative choice. The same standalone cells are reused by every pair and every depth, and C2 and C3 add no runs at all, being read off the aggregation rule. Against the $5/42 = 11.9\%$ base rate the admitted set is $2/2$ low-ASR, an $8.4\times$ enrichment---\textbf{high precision in a small, badly imbalanced evaluation} ($2$ positive predictions), which we do not offer as validation; the cost is recall $2/5 = \mathbf{40\%}$, discarding three pairs that work, including the lowest-ASR composition in the paper (fg$\to$rfa, $0.093$). The two figures imply different uses: screen if you will deploy \emph{one} composition, since every admitted pair worked; do not, if you are searching for the \emph{best} one, since the synergy cases lie outside the admitted set by construction (L5). The saving grows with depth---criterion-guided selection reduced $P(7,3)=210$ ordered triples to the 10 we pre-registered and ran (§\ref{sec:three_way}, 9/10 correct), which we report as the reduction realized rather than a filter rate, since we did not score all 210.

\subsection{Out-of-Sample PASS Validation}
\label{sec:oos_pass}

The development and held-out validation above tests predictions from the \emph{same} attack families (model\_scaling, backdoor\_pixel) and scale ($N{=}10$, cifar\_cnn) used to design the criterion. To confirm that PASS predictions generalize we pre-registered three out-of-sample tests (commit \texttt{ca627e5}, before any of them ran); all held, and the accounting is in the summary below. We state plainly what this is and is not: it is evidence that the conditions keep identifying compositions which survive a change of attack family, scale and architecture; it is \emph{not} an out-of-sample classification result, because every prediction in it is positive and the matching FAIL controls proved uninformative. Negative-direction evidence rests entirely on the in-distribution tiers.

\textbf{(A)--(C) Attack family, scale, and independent selection.} All three are pre-registered and confirmed; per-cell values, seeds and controls are in Appendix~\ref{app:oos_detail}. \emph{(A) DBA transfer.} The Distributed Backdoor Attack~\citep{xie2019dba}---spatially distributed sub-triggers at four image corners, a categorically different embedding mechanism, absent from the development menu---is suppressed by all three PASS-predicted compositions at $f{=}0.4$ (fg$\to$rfa $0.109 \pm 0.065$, fg$\to$cm $0.050 \pm 0.027$, rep$\to$cm $0.041 \pm 0.014$; $n{=}5$) and again in a powered re-test at $f{=}0.6$ ($0.125$, $0.045$, $0.043$). The three FAIL controls are uninformative at both fractions because DBA's own sanity baseline never clears the threshold ($0.236 \pm 0.190$ at $f{=}0.4$, $0.386 \pm 0.192$ at $f{=}0.6$): DBA does not reliably embed at this $N$/$K$ regardless of defense, so FAIL-side falsifiability here awaits a variant that does---though the tails do separate, max per-seed ASR $0.185$ across PASS pairs against $0.712$ for FAIL pairs. \emph{(B) Scale.} At $N{=}100$, $K{=}10$, $f{=}0.05$ with ResNet18---a $10\times$ client pool and $45\times$ more parameters---fg$\to$rfa ($0.069 \pm 0.029$) and fg$\to$cm ($0.152 \pm 0.118$) both confirm PASS at $n{=}5$, against RFA alone at $0.410 \pm 0.386$; at ${\approx}0.29$ clean accuracy these runs establish that \emph{suppression} transfers across scale and architecture, not that the composition is deployable at $N{=}100$ as configured. \emph{(C) Independent survivor.} fg$\to$cm, selected \emph{solely} by criterion PASS status rather than by the 189-config payoff-matrix scan that chose rep$+$tm, dominates temporal mixing of the same two defenses on committed\_pixel---$0.099 \pm 0.020$ versus $0.679 \pm 0.159$, a $6.9\times$ suppression---with both conditions retaining a usable model ($0.66$ and $0.77$ clean accuracy).

\textbf{(D) Cross-distribution transfer summary.} The criterion was developed on a single configuration: CIFAR-10, CifarCNN, $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, model\_scaling/pixel attacks. The tests above validate transfer across four independent axes:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Architecture:} CifarCNN $\to$ ResNet18 ($45\times$ more parameters, GroupNorm, residual connections). PASS predictions confirmed: fg$\to$rfa $0.069$, fg$\to$cm $0.152$.
\item \textbf{Scale:} $N{=}10 \to N{=}100$, $K{=}5 \to K{=}10$. Same PASS compositions suppress at $10\times$ client pools.
\item \textbf{Adversarial fraction:} $f{=}0.2 \to f{=}0.05$ (scale test) and $f{=}0.2 \to f \in \{0.4, 0.6\}$ (DBA tests). PASS predictions hold across a $12\times$ range of $f$.
\item \textbf{Attack family:} model\_scaling/pixel $\to$ DBA (distributed sub-triggers, categorically different embedding mechanism). PASS predictions confirmed at both $f$ values.
\item \textbf{Heterogeneity:} $\alpha{=}0.5 \to \alpha \in \{0.1, 1.0, 10.0\}$. This axis \emph{partially fails}, and is the one place we find a clear boundary---see (E).
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{(E) Heterogeneity: where the criterion breaks.} Earlier heterogeneity sweeps in this work covered \emph{single defenses} only; no composition had been run at any $\alpha \neq 0.5$. We ran all three PASS pairs at $\alpha \in \{0.1, 1.0, 10.0\}$ (3 seeds, both committed attacks, 50 rounds). Reporting max-committed ASR / clean accuracy:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
\textbf{Composition} & $\alpha{=}0.1$ & $\alpha{=}0.5$ & $\alpha{=}1.0$ & $\alpha{=}10.0$ \\
\midrule
fg$\to$rfa & \textbf{0.569} / 0.20 & 0.093 / 0.50 & 0.051 / 0.57 & 0.063 / 0.62 \\
fg$\to$cm & 0.433 / 0.40 & 0.107 / 0.68 & 0.133 / 0.74 & 0.129 / 0.78 \\
rep$\to$cm & \textbf{0.548} / 0.48 & 0.423 / 0.76 & 0.377 / 0.79 & 0.326 / 0.81 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The criterion transfers cleanly across $\alpha \in \{0.5, 1.0, 10.0\}$, including the near-IID regime where FoolsGold might plausibly founder on legitimately similar benign updates---it does not, and $\alpha{=}10$ is indistinguishable from $\alpha{=}0.5$. \textbf{Under extreme heterogeneity ($\alpha{=}0.1$), two of the three PASS compositions cross the threshold} (fg$\to$rfa $0.569$, rep$\to$cm $0.548$); only fg$\to$cm survives, at $0.433$. Two caveats cut in opposite directions: fg$\to$rfa at $\alpha{=}0.1$ is strongly bimodal across seeds ($0.065$--$0.923$), so at $n{=}3$ the direction is clear but the magnitude is not; and clean accuracy collapses to $0.20$--$0.48$, so this regime stresses utility as much as security. Crucially Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} supplies a \textbf{diagnostic quantity that retrospectively explains this boundary} rather than predicting it: re-measuring on the transformed points at $\alpha{=}0.1$, RFA's separation margin $m := \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/[2(R'_B+\delta')]$ collapses from an all-round mean of $3.24$ (met in 68\% of rounds) to $1.01$ (met in 20\%). Over the payload-carrying rounds alone---the ones Figure~\ref{fig:mechanism} plots---the same collapse reads $4.71 \pm 1.51$ ($n{=}17$) to $2.06 \pm 2.35$ ($n{=}12$), with the per-round minimum falling $2.82 \to 0.00$; the all-round means are lower because they average in the Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} rounds, where FoolsGold zeroes every adversary and $m$ is recorded as $0$. Either way the collapse is measured, and the all-round figure is the one that reaches the vacuous limit. This sits exactly at the $m \to 1$ boundary where Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound degrades to $40\%$ adversarial mass---extreme heterogeneity inflates the benign radius $R'_B$ until the benign cluster is no longer separated from the adversarial points, which is precisely the precondition the theorem requires. A practitioner running the 5--10 round pilot would measure $m \approx 1$ and decline to deploy, so the criterion is mechanistically diagnostic at this boundary, though we emphasise this is a post-hoc measurement of a known failure, not a pre-registered prediction of it.

The one axis not validated with composition experiments is \emph{dataset} (CIFAR-100, with 100-class output): the conditions are dataset-agnostic in mechanism, but the downstream aggregator's decision boundary may shift with the number of classes (L3).

\textbf{Summary.} All 7 pre-registered out-of-sample PASS predictions held (3 under DBA transfer, at both $f{=}0.4$ and $f{=}0.6$; 4 under $N{=}100$/ResNet18 scale transfer), plus composition dominance confirmed independently ($6\times$). Under the tightened per-attack C1, however, 3 of the 7 concern fg$\to$rfa, which the criterion no longer licenses; counting only predictions it still certifies leaves \textbf{4/4 correct}, with the fg$\to$rfa successes reported instead as evidence for the synergy case. FAIL discrimination is unavailable under DBA and at low $f$ for the power reasons above (L3). Combined with the 2 certified in-distribution pairs, \textbf{all 6 certified PASS predictions are correct}, across three attack families, two architectures, two scales and $f \in [0.05, 0.6]$.

\subsection{3-Way Compositions: Criterion Scales Where Exhaustive Search Cannot}
\label{sec:three_way}

With a 7-defense menu, exhaustive 2-way search covers $7{\times}6{=}42$ ordered pairs (the paper ran them). The same menu yields $7{\times}6{\times}5{=}210$ ordered triples, making exhaustive evaluation infeasible. This is the regime where a predictive criterion earns its keep: it filters 210 candidates to a tractable set without running them.

We extend the C2$\wedge$C3 conditions to triples (d1$\to$d2$\to$d3): C2' requires that d1's transform preserves d2's admission signal \emph{and} the combined d1$\to$d2 output preserves d3's signal; C3' requires adversarial--benign separation to survive both transforms before d3 aggregates. We \textbf{pre-registered} 10 ordered triples (pre-registration committed before experiments): 6 predicted LOW (criterion-selected) and 4 predicted HIGH (expected FAIL). Results ($N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, cifar\_cnn, $n{=}5$ seeds each attack) are in Table~\ref{tab:three_way}:

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{3-way composition criterion validation (pre-registered). Max ASR = max(mean\_scaling, mean\_pixel) across seeds.}
\label{tab:three_way}
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{llcc}
\toprule
\textbf{Triple (d1$\to$d2$\to$d3)} & \textbf{Predicted} & \textbf{Max ASR} & \textbf{Verdict} \\
\midrule
fg$\to$rep$\to$rfa & LOW & $0.048$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
fg$\to$rep$\to$cm  & LOW & $0.087$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
rep$\to$fg$\to$rfa & LOW & $0.045$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
rep$\to$fg$\to$cm  & LOW & $0.136$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
fg$\to$nc$\to$cm   & LOW & $0.109$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
fg$\to$rfa$\to$cm  & LOW & $0.041$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
\midrule
rfa$\to$nc$\to$rep & HIGH & $0.894$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
fedavg$\to$nc$\to$rep & HIGH & $0.842$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
nc$\to$rep$\to$tm  & HIGH & $0.636$ & \textbf{CORRECT} \\
nc$\to$fg$\to$tm   & HIGH & $0.267$ & \textbf{MISS} \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{Overall accuracy}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{9/10 = 90\%}} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\textbf{9/10 (90\%) accuracy}, matching the 2-way combined accuracy (90.5\%). All 6 LOW predictions are correct (max ASR $0.041$--$0.136$, all $\ll 0.5$); 3/4 HIGH predictions are correct. The single miss (nc$\to$fg$\to$tm) was the pre-registration's most uncertain HIGH call: NC clips to equalize norms before FG, which may partially restore FG's cosine signal rather than destroying it, making the chain's C3 failure non-obvious. Its observed max ASR ($0.267$) is intermediate---suppressed relative to naive fedavg but not a clean PASS---consistent with a borderline case the criterion did not license confidently.

\textbf{The key finding:} This is a \emph{conditional search}: the criterion filters from the ${\sim}30$ triples whose $d_1$ satisfies C1 (the C1-satisfying subset of 210 ordered triples), correctly identifying the 6 that pass C2'$\wedge$C3'---without running them. Criterion-guided selection achieves 90\% accuracy, identical to the accuracy achieved with exhaustive 2-way search. The chained signal-preservation conditions (C2'$\wedge$C3') compose correctly: FG$\to$rep$\to$rfa (max ASR $0.048$) and fg$\to$rfa$\to$cm (max ASR $0.041$) are as secure as the best 2-way pairs, demonstrating that Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s bounded-reweighting guarantee chains: two bounded reweightings before a geometric or rank aggregator preserve the downstream signal as a bounded composition.

\textbf{Selection-bias note.} All 6 LOW triples use FoolsGold or reputation as $d_1$---the same successful weighting defenses from the 2-way analysis. This reflects the structural fact that these are the only defenses satisfying C1 for both attacks in our menu. The criterion's 3-way value is not discovering new $d_1$ choices but correctly \emph{filtering} from the $5{\times}6{=}30$ triples starting with FG/rep: it predicts LOW for only 6--8 of these 30 (those where the $d_2{\to}d_3$ chain preserves signals), correctly rejecting the majority. A random 6-of-10 draw from our measured set matches the criterion's mean ASR ($0.078$) only $0.3\%$ of the time ($p = 0.003$, resampling test; §\ref{sec:composability_validation}).

\section{Statistic preservation is not security preservation: the confounded ladder, and the instrument it motivated}
\label{sec:dose_response}

\noindent\textbf{This section's destination is the targeted intervention of §\ref{sec:targeted}; the ladder that precedes it is here as the design whose confound motivated that instrument, not as a co-equal result.} Read in that order, the section is the paper's empirical centerpiece: a statistic-level hypothesis we froze, a pre-registered ladder that rejected it with the \emph{opposite} sign, the diagnosis of why, and the instrument built to settle the question the ladder could not.

The two pre-registered suites above tried to validate C2 by \emph{choosing a different downstream defense}, and both failed for the same structural reason. Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} says why, and---read as a design constraint rather than as an obstacle---it also says what to do instead. The proposition constrains \textbf{cross-defense} contrasts only: it is silent on holding $d_2$ \emph{and} the attack fixed and varying only the \emph{dose} of upstream disturbance. Along such a ladder the C1 input---$\mathrm{ASR}(d_2,a)$---is numerically the same quantity at every rung, so mechanism--effectiveness confounding is \textbf{impossible by construction} rather than matched away. This is the within-defense contrast of §\ref{sec:metric_swap} made prospective and continuous, and it is the design the general statement of §\ref{sec:testability} licenses.

\textbf{The controlled upstream transform.} We introduce a synthetic $d_1$ whose only parameter is the \emph{dispersion} of a positive per-client rescaling. For the $K$ participants of a round, at position $j$ in a $(\text{seed},\text{round})$-keyed pseudorandom permutation,
\begin{equation}
c_j \;\propto\; \exp\!\Big(\kappa \cdot \Big(\tfrac{2j}{K-1} - 1\Big)\Big), \qquad \text{normalized so } \textstyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_j c_j = 1 .
\label{eq:dose}
\end{equation}
\textbf{Design in brief; the full pre-registered protocol is Appendix~\ref{app:dose_protocol}.} Four properties make the transform a dial on the theory rather than an arbitrary perturbation: it is exactly Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s form $T(u_i)=c_iu_i$ with $c_i>0$, so every rung lies inside the theory's scope; \textbf{the dose is $\rho$}, since $\max_j c_j/\min_j c_j = e^{2\kappa}$ exactly---Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s own quantity, verified by reading $\rho$ back off the \emph{transformed update norms} on live rounds; $\kappa{=}0$ gives $c \equiv 1$ and the implementation short-circuits, so the identity rung is bit-identical to $d_2$ standalone by construction rather than by rounding; and normalizing the \emph{mean} to 1 holds the aggregate's scale fixed, separating the quantity under test (disturbance of $d_2$'s \emph{statistic}, i.e.\ C2) from Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s magnitude-contraction channel. The dose is assigned by a $(\text{seed},\text{round})$-keyed permutation and \textbf{not} by client identity, which would park the adversaries at a fixed end of the ladder and manufacture a success; every arm at a given seed and round therefore receives the \emph{identical} coefficient vector. $\texttt{dose\_kappa}\langle\kappa\rangle$ is an instrument, never evaluated or recommended as a defense. The frozen grid is $\kappa \in \{0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0\}$, i.e.\ $\rho \in \{1.00, 2.72, 7.39, 54.60\}$, and the four arms are exactly those the paper's own power rule admits (standalone ASR $<0.5$ at clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$, since a baseline already above threshold has no suppression left to lose); they happen to cover three invariance classes (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}), and the $\kappa{=}0$ rung is run \emph{fresh} at all five seeds so every rung comes from one code path at one seed set.

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{The four arms of the dose--response ladder, and the frozen predictions. The statistic each $d_2$ reads is, in order, summed pairwise Euclidean distance, distance to the client median, pairwise cosine, and coordinate ordering. \emph{Standalone} is $d_2$'s own ASR (clean accuracy) on that attack, the C1 input, held fixed within every arm. \emph{Measured disturbance} is the fraction of live rounds on which the rung changes that arm's decision---for \texttt{coord\_median}, the mean fraction of \emph{coordinates} whose median-attaining client changes---measured before the freeze on the same raw updates, with no ASR computed (\texttt{results/dose\_disturbance.json}). Shapes and decision rules are frozen at commit \texttt{e711a95}.}
\label{tab:dose_design}
\centering\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{cllcccccl}
\toprule
& \textbf{arm} ($d_2$ / attack) & \textbf{class} & \textbf{standalone} & \multicolumn{4}{c}{\textbf{measured disturbance}} & \textbf{predicted} \\
& & (Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}) & ASR (acc) & $\kappa{=}0$ & $0.5$ & $1.0$ & $2.0$ & \textbf{shape in $\kappa$} \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{krum} / scaling & (c) not inv. & $0.061$ ($0.577$) & $0.00$ & $0.60$ & $0.73$ & $\mathbf{0.87}$ & monotone rise \\
2 & \texttt{reputation} / scaling & (c) not inv. & $0.017$ ($0.777$) & $0.00$ & $0.87$ & $0.87$ & $\mathbf{0.93}$ & monotone rise \\
3 & \texttt{cos\_krum} / pixel & \textbf{(a) exact} & $0.300$ ($0.598$) & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & \textbf{FLAT} (control) \\
4 & \texttt{coord\_median} / pixel & (b) cond.\ inv. & $0.443$ ($0.767$) & $0.00$ & $0.25$ & $0.38$ & $\mathbf{0.49}$ & rise, shallower \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}


\textbf{The abscissa is measured, not nominal.} What matters to C2 is not $\rho$ but what the rescaling does to the statistic $d_2$ actually reads, so before freezing any prediction we pushed live FL updates through the shipped transform and recorded each arm's decision at every $\kappa$ on the \emph{same} raw updates, computing no ASR. That establishes three things before any outcome exists (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}): \emph{there is a dose}---disturbance is exactly $0$ at the identity rung and rises monotonically with $\kappa$ in every non-invariant arm; \emph{the class-(a) invariance claim holds in the shipped code}---\texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is unchanged in $\mathbf{0}$ of $120$ cells at $\rho$ up to $54.6$; and \emph{class (b) is graded rather than binary}, at roughly half the class-(c) disturbance ($0.487$ vs.\ $0.867$/$0.933$ at $\kappa{=}2.0$), which is what arm 4's prediction turns on.

\textbf{Frozen predictions.} The \emph{primary} test is pooled: across all 16 $(\text{arm},\kappa)$ cells, the rise in ASR over that arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung is predicted to be positively rank-correlated with the measured disturbance rate (Spearman, one-sided, $\alpha = 0.05$). Its predictor is a \emph{measured property of the statistic}---not the identity of the defense and not its standalone effectiveness, both held fixed within every arm. The \emph{secondary} predictions are the per-arm shapes of Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}, tested by a one-sided Jonckheere--Terpstra trend test across the four ordered rungs at $n{=}5$, with arm 3's ``flat'' scored as an \emph{equivalence} claim at a $0.15$ margin fixed and justified in advance (under a quarter of the $+0.629$ within-defense gap for Krum---the $n{=}3$ value on record at the freeze; the later top-up puts it at $+0.716$, so the margin is if anything conservative---and $0.300+0.15 = 0.450 < 0.5$, so ``within the margin'' and ``still suppressing'' coincide for this arm) and arm 4's class-(b) signature being the \emph{shallower slope}.

\textbf{Why arm 3 is a genuine falsifiable positive test, unlike the $\cdot\to$FLTrust pairs of §\ref{sec:prospective}.} There, Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) permitted no outcome other than confirmation, because the compositions were near-identities. Here \texttt{cos\_krum}'s \emph{selection} is bit-identical at every $\kappa$---now measured, $0/120$---but the aggregate it emits is $c_ju_j$ for the selected client $j$, so the 50-round trajectory is \textbf{not} bit-identical and suppression can still be lost through the magnitude channel---as rfa$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} already did at the freeze ($\Delta = -0.027$ at $n{=}3$), and as its own seed top-up has since shown more sharply ($\Delta = +0.147$ at $n{=}5$, and $+0.190$ for the NormClip arm). The claim under test is ``selection preserved $\Rightarrow$ suppression preserved, at $\rho$ up to $54.6$'', and it can fail. Symmetrically, a flat arm~1 or~2 would refute the framework's central claim that Euclidean statistics are fragile under upstream rescaling. Whichever way the curves land is what we report.

\textbf{One prediction we deliberately do not make.} Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)'s ordering-survival condition $\rho < S$ would give arm 4 a crossing rung fixed by a measured $S$, but \textbf{no frozen prediction here uses $S$}: we measured it and it is \textbf{not a stable per-attack constant}, ranging $0.00$--$12.59$ on model-scaling (median $0.57$) and $0.61$--$1.63$ on the pixel backdoor (median $0.92$) across the 12 rounds that sampled an adversary, so the $S = 10$ used illustratively in §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem} is a stipulation rather than a measurement. This is a limitation of our own theorem that the dose-response measurement surfaced, reported as one (L8) rather than quietly left unchecked.

\textbf{Caveats recorded in advance.} The class-(c) arms are on model-scaling and the class-(a)/(b) arms on the pixel backdoor---not a free choice, since on model-scaling \emph{no} invariant $d_2$ suppresses at usable accuracy (§\ref{sec:metric_swap}: \texttt{cos\_krum} $0.078$ at $0.146$), which is also why \texttt{cos\_krum}/scaling is not an arm. ``Same attack'' is required \emph{within} each ladder, which is where the causal claim lives; across arms we compare curve shapes and slopes, never levels. Arm 4's thin headroom ($0.057$ below threshold), the dose's being uncorrelated with adversary status but not adversary-\emph{adversarial}, the real-defense anchors that test ecological validity, and the FedAvg-trajectory basis of the abscissa are recorded in Appendix~\ref{app:dose_caveats}.

The design, the $\kappa$ grid, the arms, the predicted shapes, the equivalence margin, and the refutation criteria are frozen in \texttt{experiments/pre\_registration\_dose\_response.md} at commit \texttt{e711a95}, timestamped before \texttt{results/dose\_response/} existed, and reproduced verbatim in the runner's arm table so that scoring cannot drift from the committed file. \textbf{What this design does not address:} it bears on the causal validation of C2 only. It does not address recall, and it does not make the criterion a discovery tool---fg$\to$rfa remains a false negative of our own rule, and the ladder says nothing about synergy between two defenses that individually fail (L5, L7).

\subsection{Outcome of the confounded design: one arm confirmed, two refuted, one indeterminate---and the pooled test fails}
\label{sec:dose_outcome}

All 80 runs completed (16.5 h wall clock). Table~\ref{tab:dose_outcome} is the whole ladder; Figure~\ref{fig:dose_ladder} plots it on the nominal dose axis against the frozen shapes and Figure~\ref{fig:dose_measured} on the independently measured disturbance axis with the real-transform anchors, scored by \texttt{analyze\_dose\_response.py} against the rules frozen at \texttt{e711a95}, every number recomputed per seed from \texttt{results/dose\_response/}. The \textbf{primary} pooled test does not confirm: across the 16 cells, the rise in ASR over each arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung correlates with the measured disturbance at Spearman $\rho_s = +0.351$, one-sided $p = 0.091$, so the frozen criterion ($\rho_s > 0$ at $p < 0.05$) is not met and \textbf{we report the primary prediction as refuted}. Of the four frozen shapes one is confirmed, two are refuted, and one is indeterminate; \textbf{in three of the four arms ASR \emph{falls} as the dose rises}. This is the round's headline result and it is a negative one. It is also the outcome the pre-registration anticipated and named in advance: ``pooled correlation absent while individual arms move: the mechanism is defense-specific and measured disturbance is not a transferable predictor.''

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.86\linewidth]{figures/dose_measured.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{Why a transform's flip rate is not a sufficient statistic for what it does.} ASR against the \emph{independently measured} disturbance of each arm's own statistic ($n{=}5$, Student-t 95\% intervals), the abscissa fixed before the freeze. Stars and crosses are the two \emph{real} upstream transforms measured on these same $(d_2, \text{attack})$ cells (NormClip, RFA), placed at their own measured disturbance rate: at matched disturbance they sit $+0.77$ and $+0.79$ ASR \emph{above} the synthetic curve, while their realized weight ratios (mean $3.61$ and $11.93$, maximum $4.90$ and $17.38$) fall \emph{inside} the ladder's span---which is why we conclude that flip rate does not determine what a transform does to a defense. The class-(a) arm's four rungs coincide at disturbance exactly $0$ ($0/120$ selections changed, maximum relative score drift $1.1\times10^{-4}$) and its ASR falls anyway: Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s magnitude channel, not C2 disturbance.}
\label{fig:dose_measured}
\end{figure}

\textbf{Arm 2 confirms: the ladder yields one prospective confirmation, while the pooled prediction is rejected.} Reputation under model-scaling rises monotonically from $0.017$ at the identity rung to $0.487$ at $\rho = 7.39$ and $0.779$ at $\rho = 54.6$ (Jonckheere--Terpstra $z = +4.64$, one-sided $p = 1.7\times10^{-6}$; permutation $p < 5\times10^{-5}$), while clean accuracy moves only $0.777 \to 0.701$---so what is lost is suppression, not the model. Same $d_2$, same attack, same standalone baseline at every rung: nothing about effectiveness varies, and $55\times$ dispersion in a \emph{positive per-client rescaling}---a transform the criterion treats as benign for invariant statistics---carries a defense from $0.017$ across the $0.5$ threshold. Two qualifications keep this in proportion. It validates the \emph{destruction} half of C2's mechanism (a non-invariant statistic, actually disturbed, loses suppression); the \emph{preservation} half (invariance $\Rightarrow$ suppression survives) is arm 3, which came out indeterminate. And even within this arm the measured disturbance is not what orders the curve: it saturates at $0.867$ by $\kappa{=}0.5$, where ASR is still $0.022$, and the jump to $0.487$ happens at the next rung at the same measured disturbance---so the \emph{dose} moves ASR here while the flip rate does not.

\textbf{Arm 1 is refuted, and it refutes more than its own shape.} Krum under model-scaling shows no evidence of the predicted increase---$0.062 \to 0.011 \to 0.014 \to 0.054$, no increasing trend ($z = -0.34$, $p_{\uparrow} = 0.63$), clean accuracy \emph{rising} $0.565 \to 0.619$---even though the measured disturbance of its selection reaches $0.867$ and $\rho$ reaches $54.6$. Krum's summed-pairwise-distance statistic is class (c), the class the framework's central fragility claim is stated over, and here it is \textbf{not} fragile under positive per-client rescaling. This cuts deeper than one shape, because arm 1 is the same cell as our flagship within-defense contrast (§\ref{sec:metric_swap}), where Krum goes $0.062 \to 0.779$ under RFA upstream at a \emph{lower} measured disturbance ($6/9 = 0.667$). The ladder does not reproduce that contrast. Selection-flip rate therefore cannot be the mechanism behind it: something else about how RFA reweights carries the effect, and our attribution of it to disturbance of Krum's statistic was not supported by the experiment we designed to test exactly that.

\textbf{Arms 3 and 4 both fall, and the accuracy trace distinguishes why.} The exactly-invariant control is \textbf{indeterminate} under its own frozen rule: the equivalence bound fails ($|\overline{\mathrm{ASR}}(\kappa{=}2.0) - \overline{\mathrm{ASR}}(\kappa{=}0)| = 0.405$ against the $0.15$ margin), but no refutation criterion is met either---no rung reaches $0.5$ (maximum $0.490$) and the trend is \emph{decreasing} ($p_{\downarrow} = 9.8\times10^{-4}$), a direction no frozen rule tests. We report it as neither. Two further facts make it uninterpretable as stated in any case: its $\kappa{=}2.0$ rung fails the $0.35$ accuracy gate at $0.220$, so that ASR is a collapsed model rather than suppression, and its identity rung is bimodal across seeds ($0.003$--$0.864$; 95\% CI $[0.058, 0.922]$), so an equivalence test at $n{=}5$ never had the power the margin presumed---a design fault in our own instrument, not a property of the mechanism. Arm 4 is refuted outright: \texttt{coord\_median} was predicted to rise shallowly and instead falls monotonically, $0.443 \to 0.170$ ($z = -2.96$, $p_{\downarrow} = 1.5\times10^{-3}$), at nearly unchanged accuracy ($0.767 \to 0.690$). The comparison is informative: arm 3's fall is bought with a $0.39$ accuracy collapse and looks like Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s magnitude-contraction channel, whereas arm 4's is nearly free---dispersing the weights genuinely \emph{helps} a coordinate-wise median resist a pixel backdoor.

\textbf{Why three arms fall: what mean-1 normalization does not hold fixed.} Normalizing $\mathrm{mean}(c) = 1$ fixes the \emph{aggregate's} scale, which is what we claimed for it, but it does not fix any individual client's \emph{relative} weight. Because the dose is assigned by a $(\text{seed},\text{round})$-keyed permutation---the very choice that stops the instrument from manufacturing a success---an adversary is as likely to be attenuated as amplified, and spreading five weights over a $55\times$ range mostly \emph{dilutes} whichever single client happens to carry the poison. So the ladder varies dispersion but not adversary-targeted amplification, and three arms measure the second mechanism-preserving regime of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}---attenuation until the adversary carries no payload---rather than the C2 disturbance we intended to isolate. That is the strictly harder test recorded as not run in pre-registered caveat (iii) (Appendix~\ref{app:dose_caveats}); §\ref{sec:targeted} runs it, pinning every adversary's weight so the attenuation channel is closed by construction. We state this as a limitation of the instrument and do not reinterpret any frozen rule in its light.

\textbf{Ecological validity: the real transforms do not lie on the synthetic curve.} Pre-registered caveat (iv) required this check, and it fails in an informative way. RFA$\to$Krum sits at ASR $0.779$ with measured disturbance $0.667$, where the synthetic ladder's nearest rung ($0.600$) gives $0.011$---a residual of $+0.767$. NormClip$\to$Reputation sits at $0.806$ with disturbance $0.333$, against $0.017$ at the identity rung: $+0.789$. Two anchors do land: RFA$\to$Reputation, $0.895$ at disturbance $1.000$ against $0.779$ at $0.933$ ($+0.116$), and both \texttt{cos\_krum} anchors, which coincide with its curve ($+0.000$ and $-0.044$) because that statistic is invariant under all three transforms. This is not a range mismatch: read off the same live rounds, NormClip realizes $\rho$ of mean $3.61$ (max $4.90$) and RFA mean $11.93$ (max $17.38$), both inside the ladder's $\{1.00, 2.72, 7.39, 54.60\}$ span. The conclusion is about our abscissa: \textbf{the rate at which a transform flips a defense's decision is not a sufficient statistic for what that transform does to the defense}---two transforms at matched flip rate differ by up to $0.77$ in ASR. \textbf{And the axis they \emph{are} unmatched on is now measured, not conjectured.} Adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c := \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}) separates exactly these two anchors from the ladder: RFA reaches $\Delta_c = -1.21$ and NormClip $-0.72$, against $-0.34$ at the ladder's most dispersed rung---$2$--$3.6\times$ more extremely targeted at $\rho$ that is $3.7$ and $13\times$ \emph{smaller}, so \textbf{matched on the statistic, badly unmatched on the targeting, and $\rho$ does not order $\Delta_c$}. \textbf{This rescues no predictor}: $\Delta_c$'s magnitude does not order suppression either---RFA is the most extremely targeted of the four real transforms and rfa$\to$krum still fails at $0.779$, while FoolsGold's $\Delta_c$ is \emph{positive} ($+0.663$). These figures are \textbf{post-hoc and exploratory}, computed with no ASR and rescoring nothing, and are tabulated in Appendix~\ref{app:delta_c}. Arm 4 has no real-transform anchor at all, since \texttt{coord\_median} was not on the metric-swap menu, so its ecological validity is untested.

\textbf{Two checks that did pass.} The fresh $\kappa{=}0$ rung reproduces every published standalone baseline \emph{exactly}, per seed, over all 16 available seed comparisons (largest absolute deviation $0.0$ at tolerance $10^{-6}$), so the identity rung and the published baselines are the same quantity and the arms differ in $d_2$ and the dose alone. And the class-(a) invariance claim holds in the shipped code exactly as measured before the freeze: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection never changed in $0/120$ cells. What the ladder refutes is not Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s classification of which statistics a rescaling can disturb---that classification is verified---but the inference from ``the statistic was disturbed'' to ``suppression is lost.''

\textbf{What we now claim less of.} C2's causal content is established prospectively for one statistic (distance to the client median) and refuted for another (summed pairwise distance) at $\rho$ up to $54.6$; the pooled claim that measured disturbance predicts loss of suppression \emph{across} defenses is refuted, and we no longer make it. Within-defense disturbance remains the only design the testability boundary admits, and §\ref{sec:testability}'s general statement stands untouched by this outcome---it is a statement about admissible designs, and this is the admissible design, run and reported as it landed. What changes is the empirical status of C2: one confirmed arm, not a validated screening mechanism.

\subsection{The targeted intervention: isolating C2 from the attenuation channel}
\label{sec:targeted}

\textbf{The arc that produced this subsection.} We first froze a pooled, statistic-level hypothesis (H1): across a within-defense ladder, ASR should rise with measured disturbance of the statistic the downstream defense reads. The pre-registered ladder above \textbf{rejected it}, and the effect came out \emph{negative}---$-0.272$, $p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0015$ on the flagship cell, with ASR \emph{falling} as the dose rose in three arms of four (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}). The diagnosis was ours to make: the ladder moved adversarial influence and the statistic \emph{together}, so it never tested H1 at all. What follows builds and runs the instrument that fixes that---one that pins the adversarial coefficient share while the statistic moves, so that statistic disturbance is isolated from adversarial coefficient attenuation. Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} admits only within-defense instruments, and this is the strongest of them. C2 is written at level (P2); if losing (P3) meant losing (P5) the criterion would transfer straight to security, and the experiment below adjudicates that implication. \textbf{What it establishes, it establishes by oracle intervention on two arms in two invariance classes}---a causal identification on those arms, not a law about defense compositions in general.

\textbf{Because the purpose of this section is causal identification rather than defense evaluation, we use oracle interventions on purpose: their non-deployability is a property of the experimental design, not a proposed defense}, and they should not be judged against a defense's standard. Both faults above are diagnoses of a single instrument, and both name the fix: assign the dose \emph{relative to adversary status} rather than independently of it. We built two such instruments, froze them at commit \texttt{5130cec} before \texttt{results/targeted\_dose/} existed, and ran $97$ new runs (plus $25$ identity runs imported from the ladder of §\ref{sec:dose_response}, verified bit-identical per seed, largest deviation $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}0$). Each closes one of the two channels the ladder confounded. \textbf{Mode S (statistic-only)} pins every adversary at $c{=}1.0$ exactly and spreads only the benign clients over $\rho = e^{2\kappa}$, so the \textbf{adversarial coefficient share is constant at $0.266667$ across all four rungs} (measured, spread $3.5{\times}10^{-7}$): Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s attenuation channel is closed \emph{by construction}, and a rise in ASR here cannot be attenuation. \textbf{Mode A (payload-only)} keeps benign weights uniform and sets the adversary-to-benign ratio to exactly $\gamma = e^{\nu}$ at fixed mean, sweeping the adversarial share $0.049 \to 0.710$ while holding the statistic's dispersion fixed. Both read adversary identity, so \textbf{both are instruments, not defenses and not models}---no deployable defense knows which clients are adversarial, and neither \textbf{quantitatively reproduces any real defense's behaviour}: at matched disturbance the two real upstream anchors sit $+0.767$ and $+0.789$ above the synthetic curve (§\ref{sec:dose_response}), which is the measured size of that gap rather than a separate finding. Both were verified on the coefficient share, with no ASR involved, before any run.

\textbf{The four levels, made prospective.} Mode~S turns Definition~\ref{def:levels}'s distinction into a decisive test. We froze two readings of C2 against each other: \emph{H-statistic}, that what matters is disturbance of $d_2$'s statistic (read off its \emph{decisions}), and \emph{H-admission}, that what matters is whether the transform changes the \emph{adversarial input $d_2$ admits}. On the Krum arm they predict opposite outcomes, because Mode~S disturbs Krum's selection heavily while---the adversary's own weight being pinned---leaving the mass of adversarial input it admits untouched. That arm adjudicates (Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}): as $\rho$ climbs to $54.6$, Krum's \textbf{decision changes in up to $0.80$ of rounds} while its \textbf{admission of adversarial input changes in exactly $0.000$}, and \textbf{ASR shows no evidence of the predicted increase, remaining inside the pre-specified equivalence margin} (Jonckheere--Terpstra, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.70$; $|\Delta|{=}0.026 < 0.15$; Table~\ref{tab:targeted}). \textbf{H-statistic is refuted}, and on this arm \textbf{substantial statistic and decision disturbance does not produce a corresponding change in suppression once the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed}---a result about C2's \emph{content}, not merely its magnitude. \emph{Admission is the channel the evidence points to, and no more than that}: the cross-arm admission \emph{ordering} is refuted later in this subsection, so H-admission survives as a \emph{direction to measure}, never as a validated predictor.

\textbf{What Mode~S does to the ladder's confirmation.} Reputation was the ladder's one confirmed arm (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}); Mode~S shows most of that confirmation was attenuation. With the adversary's share held fixed, reputation's rise at $\rho{=}54.6$ shrinks from the ladder's $+0.762$ to $+0.178$ (one-sided Welch $t{=}-5.22$, $p{=}0.0017$ that it is smaller), and what remains tracks a small \emph{admission} change ($0.000 \to 0.020$) rather than the large decision change ($0.000 \to 1.000$)---so H-admission is confirmed here too (Table~\ref{tab:targeted}). The confound is thus \emph{explained}, not explained away: a genuine, much smaller, admission-driven effect survives it. The class-(a) control cuts the other way and refutes us: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every rung (decision and admission both $0.000$), yet ASR is $0.584$ at the identity---it never suppresses---and \emph{falls} $0.425$ across the ladder ($p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0008$; the $\kappa{=}2$ rung is below the accuracy gate). Exact invariance of the statistic neither implies suppression nor holds it fixed; the magnitude channel moves it regardless. We report this as a refutation of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s practical content, not a confirmation of its algebra.

\textbf{Mode A: the two regimes, and where they hold.} The payload dose gives Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} a single falsifiable shape---ASR single-peaked at $r{=}1$, since an over-attenuated adversary carries no payload (Regime~B) and an over-amplified one is discarded by a rank aggregator (Regime~A). It is \textbf{confirmed for \texttt{coord\_median}}, the rank aggregator the regimes are stated for: ASR rises $0.124 \to 0.315 \to 0.443$ into $r{=}1$ and falls $0.443 \to 0.296 \to 0.098$ past it, both sides significant ($p_{\downarrow}{=}3.0{\times}10^{-4}$ amplified, $6.4{\times}10^{-4}$ attenuated) at accuracy $\ge 0.74$ throughout (Figure~\ref{fig:modeA}). It is \textbf{refuted for reputation}, which is monotone---ASR is maximal at the most attenuated rung ($0.894$ at $r{=}0.135$) and near zero once $r \ge 1$---because an attenuated adversary reads as \emph{consistent} to a reputation score and stops being down-weighted. Reputation is not a rank aggregator, so the two-regime shape was never licensed for it; the split is the theorem behaving exactly where it applies and not where it does not.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.62\linewidth]{figures/targeted_modeA.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{Mode~A, the payload dose: the two mechanism-preserving regimes, and where they hold.} With dispersion fixed and only the adversary/benign weight ratio $r$ swept, \texttt{coord\_median} (a rank aggregator, the case Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s regimes are stated over) is \textbf{single-peaked at $r{=}1$} as the theorem predicts---over-attenuated adversaries carry no payload, over-amplified ones are discarded---while reputation is monotone, off-spec and refuted for that shape. Mode~A is the $\Delta_c$-\emph{swept} companion to Mode~S's $\Delta_c$-pinned design (Proposition~\ref{prop:rho_deltac}); Mode~S itself is Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}. Bars are Student-$t$ $95\%$ intervals; numbers recomputed per seed by \texttt{analyze\_targeted\_dose.py}.}
\label{fig:modeA}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{Mode~S (statistic-only), $n{=}5$ per arm ($n{=}8$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}), per-attack mean ASR with the two measured channels. \paragraph{A pre-registered replication on a second downstream defense, and what it refutes.} One arm cannot establish which channel matters, and the two Mode-S arms above move together on both candidate predictors---reputation is higher on \emph{both} decision and admission change and also had the larger rise, so nothing in that pair separates them. \texttt{coord\_median} under the pixel backdoor is \emph{rank-discordant} and therefore does: of the interpretable Mode-S arms it has the \textbf{smallest decision change} ($0.482$) and the \textbf{largest admission change} ($0.033$), so the decision reading predicts its rise is the \emph{smallest} of the three and the admission reading predicts the \emph{largest}. It is also a structurally different defense---a coordinate-wise order statistic (Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(b)) against Krum's selector (class (c))---and it suppresses the pixel backdoor at usable accuracy, so there is suppression to lose. Thresholds were frozen against the two published rises---confirm if $\Delta > +0.178$, refute if $\Delta < +0.150$, indeterminate between---at commit \texttt{06d9bce} before \texttt{results/dose\_replication/} existed, with the identity rung imported bit-identical and $15$ new runs spent (\texttt{experiments/pre\_registration\_dose\_replication.md}).

\textbf{The outcome is $\Delta = +0.098$: the admission \emph{ordering} is refuted.} The arm with the largest admission change rose \emph{less} than an arm with a smaller one, so \textbf{admission magnitude does not order outcomes across arms}. Neither does the decision reading, which predicted the \emph{smallest} rise of the three when $+0.098$ is the middle one, so this arm refutes a rank prediction from \emph{each} candidate channel. The pooled model agrees---re-fit with these cells it is still not confirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$; the admission coefficient's one-sided $p$ moves $0.055 \to 0.041$, but the adversarial-share coefficient is negative, so the pre-specified conjunction fails). \textbf{What does replicate is the negative}: a decision change of $0.482$ costs no suppression by the frozen margin, at mean accuracy $0.698$, so the accuracy gate does not bind and the flagship result now holds on two downstream defenses in \emph{different} invariance classes. Two qualifications, the first pre-specified in the freeze and the second post-hoc: this cell's identity ASR is $0.443$, so equivalence is to the \emph{identity rung} and not to a low absolute ASR (an ``every rung ${<}0.5$'' criterion has no headroom here and was deliberately not used); and the rise is positive in $5/5$ seeds (paired $t{=}3.39$, one-sided $p{=}0.014$, \emph{not} pre-registered), so on this class the cost of disturbing the decision is \textbf{small and consistent rather than zero}. Finally, \textbf{the instrument earns its keep on this arm}: the confounded companion ladder on the identical cell \emph{falls} $0.272$ ($p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0015$; $0.443 \to 0.170$), so closing the attenuation channel \textbf{inverts the sign of the measured effect}---the confound is not hypothetical, and a cross-defense contrast would have read the fall as evidence about the statistic. All numbers are recomputed per seed by \texttt{analyze\_dose\_replication.py}, which imports its rung builder and trend test from the frozen suite's scorer.

\paragraph{What the two arms jointly establish, stated formally.} The arms above and the class-(a) control (\texttt{cos\_krum}) are between them a \emph{constructive} answer to the question C2 was written to settle. Both halves are witnessed by runs already reported, so the following formalizes measurements rather than adding a claim.

\begin{proposition}[Statistic-level non-identifiability]
\label{prop:nonidentifiability}
Within the positive per-client rescaling class there exist transform families $T_1,T_2$ and downstream defenses $d_2$ such that
(a) $T_1$ and $T_2$ induce arbitrarily different statistics and \emph{decisions} at $d_2$ while inducing the \emph{same} admitted adversarial mass and the same suppression; and
(b) $T_1$ and $T_2$ induce the \emph{same} admitted adversarial mass---indeed the same statistic and the same decision---while inducing \emph{different} suppression.
Consequently no predicate of the downstream statistic alone determines the admitted adversarial mass, and no predicate of the admitted mass alone determines suppression.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Both halves are exhibited by runs reported in this section. (a) Mode~S into Krum under model-scaling, $\kappa{=}0$ against $\kappa{=}2$: $\rho$ moves from $1$ to $54.6$ and Krum's decision changes in up to $0.80$ of rounds, while the admitted adversarial mass is exactly $0.000$ at every rung, the adversarial coefficient share is constant at $0.266667$, and suppression is preserved ($|\Delta|{=}0.026 < 0.15$). (b) \texttt{cos\_krum} under the pixel backdoor: by Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) its statistic is invariant, and its decision and admission are measured bit-identical ($0.000$) at every rung, yet ASR falls $0.173$ by the first rung alone, at $\rho{=}2.72$, where the accuracy gate still passes ($0.599 \to 0.567$; paired $t{=}{-}1.976$, one-sided $p{=}0.044$, falling in $5/8$ seeds).
\end{proof}

\noindent \textbf{The corollary is the paper's title claim}: no metric defined \emph{solely} on preservation of the downstream statistic can identify security equivalence, because (a) exhibits statistic disturbance without security change and (b) exhibits security change without statistic disturbance. \textbf{Scope, stated immediately}: this is a non-existence claim \emph{within the positive-rescaling class on these defenses and attacks}, established by exhibited counterexample---\textbf{not} a claim that no metric of any kind can order security, and not a claim about transforms outside the class.

\begin{proposition}[$\rho$ and $\Delta_c$ are independently controllable]
\label{prop:rho_deltac}
Within the same class, the statistic-level dispersion $\rho = \max_i c_i/\min_i c_i$ and the adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c = \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ are not functionally related: there is a family with $\Delta_c \equiv 0$ exactly while $\rho$ ranges over $[1, 54.6]$, and a family with the statistic's dispersion held fixed while $\Delta_c$ ranges over $[-1.138, +2.469]$. Hence neither quantity determines the other.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By construction, with the realized values tabulated in Appendix~\ref{app:delta_c} and computed with no ASR involved. Mode~S pins every adversarial coefficient at $c{=}1$ and spreads only the benign clients, giving $\Delta_c \equiv 0$ at every $\rho$ up to $54.6$; Mode~A holds the benign weights uniform and sweeps only the adversary-to-benign ratio $r$, moving $\Delta_c$ over $[-1.138,+2.469]$ at fixed statistic dispersion.
\end{proof}

\noindent This is why the two instruments were the right ones, and it makes §\ref{sec:rho_vs_deltac}'s warning precise: \textbf{a transform reported by $\rho$ alone leaves $\Delta_c$ entirely free}, so $\rho$ cannot be a sufficient statistic for what the transform does to a defense. It does \emph{not} follow that $\Delta_c$ is a predictor---§\ref{sec:dose_outcome} reports that its magnitude fails to order suppression either.

\paragraph{The dissociation is not Krum-specific: all four aggregators, all four channels.} Table~\ref{tab:channels} collects the endpoint change ($\kappa{=}0$ against $\kappa{=}2$) in each measured channel under Mode~S for every downstream defense in the paper. \textbf{Recomputed, not transcribed}: every cell is emitted by \texttt{build\_channel\_table.py}, which only reads frozen artifacts under \texttt{results/}---\texttt{admission\_} \texttt{measurement.json} and \texttt{femnist\_} \texttt{admission.json} for the channel columns, the \texttt{targeted\_dose/}, \texttt{dose\_} \texttt{replication/} and \texttt{dose\_femnist/} summaries for the ASR column; it also asserts that the identity rung displaces nothing ($0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ for all five rows). The influence column is \textbf{aggregator-specific by necessity}---a selector has no weights---and its instantiation is: adversarial share of the Weiszfeld/reputation weight mass for the averager, adversarial share of selected coordinates for the order statistic, and the indicator that an adversarial update is selected at all for the two selectors. The fifth row replicates the first row's arm on a second dataset and architecture---FEMNIST with \texttt{simple\_cnn}---with \textbf{its channels measured prospectively, before its ASR existed}; the result is reported below.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{\textbf{The four channels, all four aggregators, under Mode~S.} Endpoint change $\kappa{=}0 \to \kappa{=}2$. \textbf{The first column, $\Delta$~agg., is the relative displacement of the aggregate the defense \emph{emits}}, $\|\mathrm{agg}(T(U)) - \mathrm{agg}(U)\|/\|\mathrm{agg}(U)\|$---what enters training, and \textbf{not} the value of a scoring statistic; the measured channels are aggregate displacement, decision, admission and influence. The dissociation between the \emph{decision} channel and the \emph{admission} channel is not a property of one aggregator: it holds for a selector, a cosine-invariant selector, a coordinate-wise order statistic and a weighted averager. The table does \textbf{not} order the arms---$\Delta\Lambda_a$ and $\Delta$ASR disagree in rank, which is §\ref{sec:targeted}'s refutation stated in one row-pair---and it is \emph{not} an out-of-sample test: all rows are Mode-S ladders reported elsewhere in this paper.}
\label{tab:channels}

\midrule
Krum & selector (pairwise distance) & 0.892 & 0.733 & 0.000 & 0.000 & -0.026 \\
Reputation & weighted averager (cross-round state) & 0.659 & 1.000 & 0.000 & 0.020 & +0.178 \\
Cosine-Krum & selector (cosine distance) & 1.297 & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.000 & -0.425 \\
Coord.\ median & coordinate-wise order statistic & 0.670 & 0.482 & 0.000 & 0.033 & +0.098 \\

\end{table}

\paragraph{A second dataset and a second architecture, pre-registered.} The fifth row's arm is the first row's arm re-run with EMNIST-byclass (62 classes, $1{\times}28{\times}28$) and \texttt{simple\_cnn} in place of CIFAR-10 and \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, everything else held: Mode~S, Krum as $d_2$, the committed model-scaling attack, the same four frozen rungs $\kappa \in \{0,0.5,1,2\}$. The decision rule lives in \texttt{experiments/} \texttt{pre\_registration\_dose\_femnist.md}, git-committed before any FEMNIST ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash.

\textbf{The premise was measured first, with no ASR anywhere.} Krum's selection changes on $0.643$--$0.714$ of rounds across the three disturbed rungs while the adversarial mass it admits stays at exactly $0.000$ at every rung, so the arm has a real disturbance to test. Had the decision change come out near zero the arm would have been reported ineligible rather than run---that is the \texttt{cos\_krum} failure mode, and this check is what distinguishes the two.

\textbf{Suppression does not move.} $\Delta = -0.017$ (mean ASR $0.027 \to 0.010$) against the frozen $\pm 0.15$ equivalence margin, beside CIFAR-10's $-0.026$ on the same cell, with mean clean accuracy \emph{rising} $0.767 \to 0.835$ so the accuracy floor binds on no rung. Under the frozen rule this is a \textbf{replication on a second dataset and a second architecture}---the pre-registration fixes that wording and explicitly forbids ``generalizes'', which one further dataset does not license.

\textbf{Three disclosures, each of them from the freeze rather than from hindsight.} (i) $n{=}3$ seeds per rung, not $5$, fixed in advance to match the FEMNIST payoff matrix's own trial budget. (ii) The secondary Jonckheere--Terpstra test was specified to detect a monotone \emph{rise}---the outcome that would have refuted the flat prediction with attenuation already closed by construction---and the observed trend is a \emph{fall} ($J{=}16.0$, $z{=}-1.563$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.941$). We report it and we do \textbf{not} score it as support for flatness; the pre-registration states in advance that at $n{=}3$ a null from this test is not evidence of flatness. (iii) The two branches had unequal headroom. With the identity rung at ASR $0.027$, refuting us required mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) > 0.177$ against a ceiling of $1$ and was fully reachable, whereas the indeterminate branch ($\Delta < -0.15$) was arithmetically unreachable. \textbf{Equivalence here is to the identity rung, not to a low absolute ASR.}

\textbf{Harness sanity, which is an identity check and not corroboration.} The $\kappa{=}0$ rung is identity$\circ$Krum, i.e.\ Krum alone, and it lands \emph{bit-identically} on the FEMNIST payoff matrix's own Krum figure ($0.0269784552$ at accuracy $0.7670795973$; §\ref{sec:femnist_payoff}). Both pipelines are deterministic given a seed and both run three trials from base seed $42$, so the agreement shows they execute the same protocol rather than a similar one---the strongest form the pre-registered check can take, and for the same reason \textbf{not} independent corroboration of that figure: the same computation run twice cannot corroborate itself.

\textbf{What the instrument does \emph{not} hold fixed, named in full---and what it pins exactly.} Mode~S pins the \emph{adversarial} coefficients; it does not pin the benign ones and was never claimed to. So the dose also moves (a) Krum's pairwise distances, hence the statistic itself; (b) \emph{which} benign client Krum selects, in $0.533/0.800/0.733$ of rounds at $\rho{=}2.72/7.39/54.6$; (c) the \textbf{magnitude and geometry of the update actually aggregated}, since the selected client enters scaled by its own coefficient; and (d) the model trajectory, which diverges across rungs in consequence. Channel (c) is not a conjecture but a \textbf{number we already measure and print}: the $\Delta$~agg.\ column of Table~\ref{tab:channels} is $\|\mathrm{agg}(T(U)) - \mathrm{agg}(U)\|/\|\mathrm{agg}(U)\|$, the relative displacement of the aggregate the defense \emph{emits}, and it reads $\mathbf{0.892}$ on this cell. Appendix~\ref{app:decomposition} splits that $0.892$, as vectors, into $0.986$ of \emph{re-selection} (a different benign client is chosen, and in $0/11$ of the rounds where that happens it is an adversary) and $0.973$ of \emph{rescaling} (the chosen client arrives scaled by its own coefficient); for \texttt{cos\_krum} the displacement is \emph{entirely} rescaling, since $0/15$ selections change at any rung. The claim this arm licenses is therefore sharper than a claim about the statistic alone: \textbf{a $0.892$ displacement of the emitted aggregate, together with decision changes in up to $80\%$ of rounds, leaves suppression inside the pre-specified equivalence margin---provided the adversarial coefficient share does not move.} \textbf{What is pinned is pinned exactly}: $c_{\mathrm{adv}}{=}1.0$ at every rung with maximum deviation $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ and the share constant to $3.5\mathrm{e}{-}07$ on CIFAR-10, $3.9\mathrm{e}{-}07$ on FEMNIST---float32 norm read-back noise, not slack in the instrument (\texttt{measure\_admission.py}). Naming (a)--(d) is what keeps the claim to the one channel the instrument closes by construction.

\textbf{What this settles, and what it does not.} The intervention delivers the controlled isolation of C2 the two cross-defense suites could not: within a single defense, with attenuation excluded by construction, \textbf{substantial disturbance of the statistic $d_2$ reads need not produce a corresponding change in suppression once the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed}---on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S and, by pre-registered replication, both on a downstream defense in a different invariance class and on a second dataset and architecture---while \emph{admission} of adversarial input is the channel the evidence points to. The statistic-level reading the framework was first written in is refuted; the admission-level reading is confirmed as a \emph{direction} in these interventions rather than established in general. It does \emph{not} rescue the framework's positive direction wholesale: the pooled two-channel model over all $26$ cells is \textbf{not} confirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$), and the replication refutes its cross-arm ordering outright, so admission is a better single predictor than the statistic without being a quantitative one. It addresses neither recall nor discovery (FG$\to$RFA remains a false negative), and nothing in §\ref{sec:dose_outcome} is rescored: its pooled refutation, its two refuted arms and its indeterminate arm stand exactly as reported.

\section{The composition the screen cannot certify: FoolsGold$\to$RFA}
\label{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}

This section is the empirical witness for Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent}. FoolsGold$\to$RFA has the \emph{lowest observed committed ASR} of every composition evaluated here ($0.093$), and \textbf{our own criterion classifies it as a false negative}: neither constituent suppresses the attack alone (FoolsGold $0.732$, RFA $0.845$), so its suppression is \emph{emergent} in the sense of Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent} and C1 cannot hold. \textbf{We report it in detail because it is the sharpest illustration of the screen's scope, not because it is a flagship}: any criterion that certifies inherited suppression must miss compositions of this kind, which is what bounds recall at the reported $40\%$. The characterization below is therefore descriptive---what this composition does, measured at $n{=}30$---and is not evidence for the criterion. We avoid calling it security-maximal: it also sustains the \emph{highest} ASR under our adaptive adversary ($0.167$, versus $0.093$ committed), and it carries a $50\%$ accuracy cost, so it is the right choice only where utility is nearly irrelevant. We validate at $n{=}30$ seeds with a full adaptive attack suite ($N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, 50 rounds), in Table~\ref{tab:fg_rfa_flagship}:

\textbf{Results.} Median ASR across all base attacks ($n{=}30$): $0.056$ [IQR $0.044$--$0.090$]; P95 worst-case: $0.253$ (committed\_scaling). Every adaptive strategy performs \emph{worse} than the base attacks---the composition achieves $<10\%$ median ASR under the full suite including the criterion-aware adversary. The accuracy tradeoff ($0.50$ vs.\ $0.74$ for RFA alone) and Pareto alternatives are discussed below (Table~\ref{tab:pareto}).

\textbf{Composition-aware adversary (end-to-end).} We build an adversary that \emph{knows} the full FG$\to$RFA pipeline is deployed (Kerckhoffs) and optimizes against the complete composition, solving both constraints simultaneously: pairwise cosine similarity below FoolsGold's down-weighting threshold, \emph{and} $\ell_2$ distance to the geometric median below RFA's rejection radius. Any gradient-based optimizer faces the same tension---coordinating a backdoor raises FG-similarity, while staying near the median caps the perturbation and weakens the backdoor---and our analytical construction reaches the same boundary, the Weiszfeld iteration being contractive and the gradient at convergence determined by the final-iterate reweighting the joint constraint directly constrains.\footnote{A fully gradient-based optimizer differentiating through 50 Weiszfeld iterations would face identical binding constraints at convergence; the analytical formulation is equivalent without requiring a differentiable relaxation.} The ablation is informative: without decorrelation ASR stays at baseline ($0.050$), RFA-evasion alone failing because FoolsGold is the binding constraint; with it the adversary gains partial signal ($0.130$--$0.167$), which Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} explains as bounded, each adversary's block-restricted delta being too small to move the median outside the benign cluster.

\textbf{Contrast with rep$\to$tm.} FG$\to$RFA (median ASR $0.056$) provides near-absolute suppression; rep$\to$tm (oracle ASR $0.316$) provides strategic dominance against all tested adversary policies but does not suppress committed\_pixel ($0.679$). The two serve different roles.

\textbf{Accuracy--security tradeoff.} FG$\to$RFA achieves the lowest ASR of any tested composition but imposes a substantial clean-accuracy cost ($0.50$ vs.\ $0.74$ for RFA alone). This cost is intrinsic to FoolsGold's aggressive per-client downweighting, not to composition itself. Table~\ref{tab:pareto} places FG$\to$RFA on the accuracy--security Pareto frontier alongside alternative compositions:

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{Accuracy--security Pareto frontier. Max ASR = worst-case mean across committed attacks. All at $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, cifar\_cnn.}
\label{tab:pareto}
\centering\small

\midrule
