\noindent FG$\to$RFA is the correct choice under a \emph{security-first} objective (e.g., safety-critical FL where any backdoor is unacceptable). For operators with accuracy constraints, FG$\to$CM (acc $0.68$, max ASR $0.107$) provides a Pareto-superior tradeoff: statistically indistinguishable suppression (Welch $p = 0.52$) at $37\%$ more accuracy. Rep$\to$CM (acc $0.76$, max ASR $0.423$) offers near-baseline accuracy with moderate security. The criterion certifies FG$\to$CM and Rep$\to$CM; FG$\to$RFA reaches the lowest ASR of the three but fails the tightened per-attack C1 and is therefore a false negative of our own criterion rather than a certified recommendation. The choice among them is an operator-specific utility decision, not a criterion failure.

\section{Why Composition, Not Mixing}
\label{sec:framework}

\textbf{This section is motivation, not evidence for the criterion.} It answers a prior question---why study \emph{sequential} composition at all, rather than randomizing the defense across rounds---and its result is independent of everything the rest of the paper argues: persistent state combined with non-erasing defenses makes per-round aggregator switching lose its information value, so mixes become max-like and deterministic composition dominates. That establishes where composition is the relevant object; it establishes nothing about which compositions preserve a mechanism, and no part of the preservation hierarchy or the identification boundary rests on it. Figure~\ref{fig:phase_boundary} plots the settings we evaluated in the two coordinates that organize them; it draws no regimes and locates no boundary.

\begin{figure}[h]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.80\linewidth]{figures/phase_boundary.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{The evaluated settings, plotted. No regimes, and one line, which is a measurement.} Each marker is a setting we ran, placed in the two coordinates that organize them: persistence retention $\gamma$ against the best single constituent defense's ASR, $p_{\mathrm{eff}}$. At $\gamma{=}0$, the stateless spam game, where randomization has value (VoPD $=0.200$). At the measured FL persistence $\hat\gamma \approx 0.66$ (the dotted line): the NC/fedavg temporal mix at ASR $0.956$, the C1-FAIL Rep$+$TM pair, and the two criterion-PASS compositions. Each point carries its own measured value as its label. \textbf{An earlier version of this figure shaded four regime bands and drew threshold guides at $\gamma{=}0.15$, $p_{\mathrm{eff}}{=}0.3$ and $p_{\mathrm{eff}}{=}0.6$; none of those three numbers was measured, so all six are gone.} Five points cannot locate a boundary, and nothing here is evidence of a phase transition in $(\gamma, p_{\mathrm{eff}})$ space.}
\label{fig:phase_boundary}
\end{figure}

The composability criterion (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}) tells practitioners \emph{which} compositions work; this section establishes \emph{why} the alternative---temporal mixing (randomly selecting one defense per round)---fails. The full game-theoretic framework (utility functions, equilibrium analysis, VoPD formalism, sample complexity, and the persistence Markov model) appears in Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework}; here we present only the key result and its mechanism.

\textbf{Persistence makes mixing max-like (in the persistent regime).} Once a backdoor trigger embeds (via model-scaling or pixel poisoning), it persists across rounds with retention $\gamma \approx 0.66$. Intermittently sampling a suppressing defense cannot remove an already-embedded trigger. The temporal mix therefore converges to the ASR of its \emph{worst constituent}---max-like rather than average-like behavior. Only at $\gamma{=}0$ (stateless threats, e.g.\ spam) does randomization pay off (VoPD $= 0.200$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended}).

\textbf{Composition dominates on the rep+tm menu.} On a menu where reputation kills scaling (ASR $0.017$) and trimmed\_mean partially suppresses pixel (ASR $0.62$), the server can mix or compose. Result ($n{=}30$):

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Adversary policy & Composition (rep+tm) & Temporal mix & Best pure defense \\
\midrule
committed\_scaling & $\mathbf{0.023}$ & $0.871$ & $0.017$ (reputation) \\
committed\_pixel   & $0.679$ & $0.747$ & $0.616$ (trimmed\_mean) \\
oracle             & $\mathbf{0.316}$ & $0.915$ & $0.519$ (coord\_median) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Against an oracle adversary, composition achieves ASR $0.316$ vs.\ temporal mix $0.915$---a $3\times$ improvement. No single defense achieves low ASR against both attacks simultaneously; composition applies both mechanisms every round. FG$\to$RFA (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}) provides even stronger suppression (max ASR $0.093$) via the theorem-backed mechanism of §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem}.

\textbf{Scale validation ($N{=}100$, ResNet18).} At $N{=}100$, $K{=}10$, $f{=}0.05$, ResNet18 ($n{=}10$): rep+tm composition suppresses all attacks to ${\approx}0.10$ ASR while temporal mixing yields committed-scaling $0.410$ (bimodal; VoPD $= -0.262$, oracle \emph{hurts itself} by switching). The qualitative result transfers to $10\times$ scale with a deeper architecture (full table: Appendix~\ref{app:oos_pass}).

\textbf{Compute-matched control: order matters, not compute.} Composition applies two mechanisms every round while temporal mixing applies one, which would confound ``two defenses vs.\ one'' with ``deterministic vs.\ randomized.'' We resolve it directly, comparing fixed FG$\to$CM against a \emph{randomized-order} variant that applies both defenses every round at equal compute (50/50 FG$\to$CM vs.\ CM$\to$FG). The criterion predicts the randomized variant degrades, because CM$\to$FG rounds degenerate to FG-aggregation alone---CM is a coordinate-wise aggregator, not a per-client transform, so with $d_1{=}$CM the downstream FG sees unmodified updates. At $n{=}5$, $N{=}10$, $f{=}0.2$: fixed FG$\to$CM reaches committed\_pixel ASR $0.076 \pm 0.035$ against the randomized variant's $0.681 \pm 0.133$, a $9\times$ degradation at identical per-round compute; for FG$\to$RFA the gap is larger still ($0.037 \pm 0.011$ vs.\ $0.719 \pm 0.113$). The advantage is structural: C2$\wedge$C3 are order-sensitive (FG$\to$CM PASS, CM$\to$FG fails), so randomizing the order randomizes whether the signal-preservation conditions hold at all, not merely which defense runs.

\textbf{Near-optimality of committed strategies.} Stronger-than-oracle adversaries (\emph{commit-then-probe}, \emph{belief-state}) at $N{=}50$/$f{=}0.05$ gain only $+0.034$ and $+0.044$ over committed play ($n{=}30$ each)---both below the POMDP switching bound of $0.048$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:pomdp_bound}, Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework}).

\textbf{Summary.} Persistence converts a signal-preservation failure (broken C2/C3) into total failure by accumulating admitted contributions: temporal mixing cannot clear an embedded trigger, whereas composition prevents embedding in the first place by applying both mechanisms every round. The game-theoretic analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework}) formalizes this---realized VoPD $\approx 0$ on all tested menus ($N \in \{10,20,50,100\}$), with the persistence boundary separating regimes where mixing could conceivably help (stateless, $\gamma{=}0$) from the FL regime where it cannot ($\gamma \gg 0$).

\section{Related Work}
\label{sec:related}

\noindent\textbf{The contrast in one sentence.} Prior work asks whether a defense withstands an attack. We ask whether an \emph{evaluation criterion} can causally attribute the security of a composition to preservation of the downstream mechanism.

\textbf{Byzantine-robust aggregation, studied in isolation.} Krum~\citep{blanchard2017machine}, Trimmed Mean~\citep{yin2018byzantine}, RFA~\citep{pillutla2022robust}, FoolsGold~\citep{fung2020foolsgold}, FLTrust~\citep{cao2021fltrust} and FLAME~\citep{nguyen2022flame} are each proposed and evaluated as a \emph{single} stage. We propose no new defense; we reason about \emph{ordered pairs} of existing ones, which is a different object---the upstream stage's output is the downstream stage's input, so the question is no longer whether a mechanism works but whether it \emph{survives} a transform applied before it. Ensemble adversarial training~\citep{tramer2018ensemble} \emph{assumes} that upstream processing preserves downstream signals; we make that assumption explicit, checkable, and---as it turns out---\textbf{the wrong abstraction for security}.

\textbf{Adaptive attacks are why an inherited-robustness guarantee is not a security guarantee.} Optimized model poisoning defeats Krum, trimmed mean and FoolsGold once the attack is chosen \emph{against} the defense~\citep{shejwalkar2021manipulating,bhagoji2019analyzing}, and the same holds for distributed and durable backdoors~\citep{xie2019dba,wang2020attack,zhang2022neurotoxin}. Our C1 quantifies over a \emph{committed} attack by construction, so this literature bounds what any inherited-suppression criterion---ours included---can promise. Our single whitebox composition-aware stress test (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}) is reported in order to \emph{measure} that gap ($0.051 \to 0.167$ seed-matched, $3.3\times$), not to defend the criterion against it; a screening condition stated against an adaptive adversary is what the framework most lacks.

\textbf{Evaluation methodology, which is where this paper sits.} The adjacent precedent is adversarial robustness, where reported numbers proved unreliable until evaluations were required to be adaptive~\citep{croce2020robustbench}. Our point is methodological in the same way but about \textbf{identification} rather than attack strength: composition studies gate candidate pairs on standalone effectiveness~\citep{kairouz2021advances} and then read the composition's outcome as evidence about the \emph{mechanism}, which Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} shows cannot work at \emph{any} sample size---the counterfactual is excluded by the gate, not undersampled. Statements of a composition-preservation intuition in that literature are informal; what is new here is the \emph{level hierarchy} at which the intuition fails (Definition~\ref{def:levels}) and the class of designs that can test it (§\ref{sec:testability}, §\ref{sec:targeted}). The causal-inference template we invoke---conditioning on a common descendant of treatment and outcome---is standard outside this literature~\citep{elwert2014endogenous,pearl2009causality}; what is not standard is noticing that FL composition studies instantiate it, because the gate is presented as a reasonableness filter (``we study defenses that work'') rather than as conditioning.

\textbf{Attacks and defenses.} Backdoor attacks~\citep{chen2017targeted,bagdasaryan2020backdoor,wang2020attack,xie2019dba} and robust defenses~\citep{pillutla2022robust,sun2019can,blanchard2017machine} form the landscape. Beyond our norm-based menu, certified robustness~\citep{xie2021crfl} and filtering defenses~\citep{nguyen2022flame,fung2020foolsgold} build stronger single mechanisms; our negative result is menu-relative and shows that \emph{randomizing over} a norm-based menu adds no value while deterministic composition dominates. Randomized transformations~\citep{xie2018mitigating,liu2018towards} in centralized settings are the closest conceptual neighbors; we provide a condition (persistence boundary) for when such randomization retains value in FL.

\textbf{Backdoor persistence and durability.} The persistence mechanism we formalize has empirical precursors. \citet{bagdasaryan2020backdoor} design model-scaling explicitly to embed triggers that survive gradient updates; \citet{zhang2022neurotoxin} (Neurotoxin) engineer triggers to occupy ``less-used'' gradient directions, achieving multi-round persistence even under strong norm clipping. These works establish the phenomenon; our contribution is a formal $(\gamma, \alpha, p_{\mathrm{eff}})$ model that quantifies when persistence eliminates the value of defense switching, and the boundary condition (low $p_{\mathrm{eff}}$) under which it would not. Notably, Neurotoxin's design targets exactly the parameter regime ($\gamma \to 1$) that our Heuristic~1 identifies as collapsing randomization value.

\textbf{Moving Target Defense (MTD).} Our result can be read as a negative boundary for MTD-style FL approaches: in stateful threat models where triggers persist across rounds, randomizing over defense implementations does not provide the diversity benefit that motivates MTD~\citep{tambe2011security}. The positive case (VoPD $= 0.200$ in our spam control, $\gamma{=}0$) shows MTD-style randomization \emph{can} help when the threat model is stateless; the FL regime's persistence is what eliminates the benefit.

\textbf{Defense composition and ensembling.} Composing defenses is well-studied in adversarial ML for centralized models: adversarial training combined with input purification~\citep{croce2020robustbench}, detection-then-rejection pipelines, and ensemble adversarial training~\citep{tramer2018ensemble}. These works implicitly require that upstream processing does not destroy downstream signals---our signal-preservation criterion formalizes this requirement explicitly with measurable margins. In Byzantine-tolerant distributed systems, protocol composition (e.g., layered BFT) has a long history; the key distinction is that Byzantine agreement composes for fault tolerance among replicas, while our setting composes for poison resilience across client updates where the adversarial signal is a continuous gradient, not a binary fault. To our knowledge, no prior work provides a predictive criterion for which FL defense compositions succeed.

\textbf{Value of information and security games.} VoPD instantiates classical VOI~\citep{howard1966information}; Blackwell~\citep{blackwell1953equivalent} provides the general characterization. Security games~\citep{tambe2011security} use Stackelberg formulations (our setting is simultaneous). Empirical game theory~\citep{wellman2006methods,walsh2002analyzing} constructs payoff matrices via simulation---our methodology. \citet{shejwalkar2021manipulating} motivate the information-asymmetric framing.

\section{Limitations}
\label{sec:limitations}

\textbf{(L1) Theorem~\ref{thm:complementarity} instantiates classical VOI.} The underlying result is Howard~1966; our contribution is the algorithmic checkability, sample-complexity bound, and deployment relevance --- not an independent theoretical advance.

\textbf{(L2) The persistence heuristic is qualitative} (${\sim}20\times$ slack). Out-of-sample MAE $= 0.041$ (NormClip) and $0.059$ (NE3) conditional on admissibility (Appendix~\ref{app:persistence_admissibility_v3}).

\textbf{(L3) Limited scope and architecture-conditional boundaries.} Three attack families (model\_scaling, pixel, DBA), two architectures (CifarCNN, ResNet18, plus \texttt{simple\_cnn} on the replicated arm), and one dataset (CIFAR-10) for every composition suite but one. The collapse boundary is architecture-conditional (ResNet18 suppression at NC10/rep90: $0.865$ vs.\ CifarCNN $0.097$, a $10\times$ shift), so results at other $N$, $K$ or datasets may differ. Cross-distribution transfer is validated across architecture, scale ($N \in \{10,100\}$) and adversarial fraction ($f \in [0.05,0.6]$; §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(D)), and \emph{dataset} transfer is now tested for the flagship Mode-S arm alone---EMNIST-byclass with \texttt{simple\_cnn}, where the negative replicates at $\Delta = -0.017$ (§\ref{sec:targeted})---while remaining untested for the 42-pair screen and for every other composition suite: the signal-preservation conditions are dataset-agnostic in mechanism (cosine similarity, geometric distance), yet the downstream aggregator's decision boundary may shift with output dimensionality. \emph{What transfers:} the qualitative PASS/FAIL prediction, the mechanism, and the ordering (composition dominates mixing). \emph{What does not:} the exact suppression ASR. A practitioner at $N{=}10^4$ should use the criterion to select candidates and then run pilot evaluations at their target scale---the criterion identifies \emph{what to try}, architecture-specific calibration determines \emph{how well it works}.

\textbf{(L4) Selection bias.} Rep30/tm70 is the arg-max of 189 configurations; the per-seed-max estimate $+0.032$ is winner-biased (54\% shrinkage from the 5-seed pilot). FoolsGold+TM (rank 5 from the same scan; Appendix~\ref{app:game_theory_framework}) provides a within-scan out-of-family check: per-seed-max VoPD $+0.082$ ($p{=}0.007$). Because composition dominates in both cases, selection bias affects only the strength of the existence proof, not the negative main result.

\textbf{(L5) The composability criterion is an evaluation-prioritization rule, not a security screen and not a discovery tool.} \textbf{It has no established predictive validity}: it orders candidates for evaluation, and the pooled model that would let it predict is unconfirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$) with its cross-arm ordering refuted (§\ref{sec:targeted}). The criterion gives \emph{sufficient} conditions (C1$\wedge$C2, with C3 as a margin diagnostic): it reliably identifies mechanism-preserving compositions in the common case where one defense is known-effective, but it cannot certify synergistic compositions where no single defense suppresses the attack alone. The conservative bias is by design---false negatives are operationally cheap to recover from (try the composition anyway) while false positives would deploy an insecure stack. Accuracy is 16/18 $=$ 89\% on the development set, 22/24 $=$ 92\% on the pre-registered held-out set (90.5\% combined 2-way) and 9/10 $=$ 90\% on pre-registered 3-way triples (§\ref{sec:three_way}). All clean C1$\wedge$C2$\wedge$C3 PASS predictions are correct; the four misses are either the predicted intermediate behavior of rank-based aggregators (Proposition~\ref{prop:rank_invariance}: dev-set NC$\to$cm, rfa$\to$cm; held-out fedavg$\to$cm) or one speculative LOW call the criterion does not license (held-out fg$\to$NC, a C1 failure). Formalization is partial: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} proves C2$\wedge$C3 for the weighting$\to$rank/geometric class with explicit margins, the remaining families are empirically validated, and C3's threshold condition depends on $d_2$'s discrimination boundary, which in practice requires calibration runs.

\textbf{(L6) Per-round aggregator switching only.} We test per-round defense selection. Randomized \emph{internals} (DP noise, subsampling smoothing à la CRFL, random participation masks) modify the embedding mechanism directly and may retain value even under persistence. Three adaptive attacks are tested against composition: projection-based (best ASR $0.597$ at $\epsilon{=}1$), Neurotoxin-style~\citep{zhang2022neurotoxin} top-$k$ gradient projection, and a two-phase consensus-shifting attack against rep$+$tm that biases updates toward the target at rate $r$ for 25 rounds before injecting full backdoor updates (§\ref{sec:composition_rep_tm}). At shift\_rate $\in \{0.01,0.05\}$, $n{=}10$ each: mean ASR $0.684 \pm 0.138$ and $0.658 \pm 0.144$, comparable to committed\_pixel ($0.679$) and \emph{below} the temporal mix ($0.915$). At $f{=}0.2$ the honest $80\%$ anchor the coordinate-wise median, limiting Phase-1 drift to ${\sim}r/(1{-}f)$ per round, so Phase 2 degenerates to standard committed\_pixel---a mechanism that weakens monotonically with $f$, and at $f \leq 0.05$ over $T \geq 200$ rounds cumulative drift may eventually overcome it. The robustness claim is therefore specific to the tested regime ($f{=}0.2$, $T{=}50$).

\textbf{(L7) No falsifiable prospective positive evidence, and C0 was added retrospectively.} This is the sharpest limitation we can state about our own design, and we state it rather than let a reader find it. Of the prospective suite's 8 predicted-LOW pairs (§\ref{sec:prospective}), 6 are $\cdot\to$FLTrust compositions that are near-\emph{identities}: FLTrust gates on a scale-invariant cosine and renormalizes every update to the server-gradient norm, discarding exactly the magnitude information an upstream positive rescaling modifies (verified bit-identical per seed for pass-through and non-clipping $d_1$). Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) permits no other outcome, so these confirmations cannot falsify it; the remaining 2 have FLTrust as $d_1$, where suppression is inherited and C0 puts them out of scope. Two further caveats: C0 was formulated \emph{after} seeing this suite, so its 7 exclusions are retrospective rather than pre-registered; and the three matched contrasts that most directly probe Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} (a $19$--$20\times$ ASR gap between $\cdot\to$FLTrust and $\cdot\to$Krum at fixed $d_1$) confound C1 with C2, because in our menu the invariant-signal $d_2$ is also the only $d_2$ effective alone. We had named the decisive experiment as a downstream defense with a rescaling-invariant admission signal that is \emph{weak in isolation}; Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} shows no such pair can be certified in scope, so that experiment does not exist---the confounding is structural, not a gap in our menu. §\ref{sec:metric_swap} runs the achievable substitute, holding the suppression \emph{principle} fixed while varying only the invariance class, and it did not deliver a positive test either: standalone effectiveness could not be held fixed, because for this defense family the magnitude information the mirrors discard is the same information the originals use to suppress a magnitude attack ($8/8$ frozen labels, TP${}=0$). So after two pre-registered attempts the criterion's positive direction still rests on in-distribution and out-of-sample PASS evidence, and the strongest C2-specific evidence in the paper is the \emph{post-hoc} within-defense contrast of §\ref{sec:metric_swap}, which holds C1 exactly fixed but was not pre-registered and is statistically resolved on only one of its two cells at $n{=}3$. §\ref{sec:dose_response} is the pre-registered design that answers this, and it delivers exactly one falsifiable prospective positive result---arm 2, reputation under model-scaling, $0.017 \to 0.779$ across the dose at $p = 1.7\times10^{-6}$ with clean accuracy nearly unchanged---while refuting two of its own four predictions and its pooled prediction. So the honest state of the positive direction after the ladder is: one confirmed arm, on the \emph{destruction} half of C2's mechanism, with the preservation half's designated control indeterminate (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}). The targeted intervention of §\ref{sec:targeted} advances this without closing it: closing the attenuation channel by construction, it isolates C2 within a single defense---substantial disturbance of the statistic does not produce a corresponding change in suppression \emph{on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S}, with \emph{admission} of adversarial input the channel the evidence points to there (H-statistic refuted, H-admission confirmed on that arm, pooled model not confirmed)---and it \emph{refutes} the preservation-half reading the ladder left open, since the exactly-invariant \texttt{cos\_krum} neither suppresses nor holds ASR fixed. A third pre-registered attempt (§\ref{sec:targeted}) was designed to be the falsifiable prospective \emph{positive} test: a replication arm chosen because the statistic reading and the admission reading order its rise \emph{oppositely}, with the ordering frozen in git before the arm was run. It replicated the negative on a defense in a different invariance class and \textbf{refuted the positive ordering}---the arm with the largest measured admission change rose \emph{less} ($+0.098$) than an arm whose admission change was smaller. So what remains absent is still a validated \emph{positive} predictor: after that replication the pooled two-channel model is not confirmed over $26$ cells ($R^2 = 0.239$) and its cross-arm ordering is refuted outright, so the criterion's positive direction is unestablished as a screen, and admission is a better \emph{direction} than the statistic without being a quantitative one.

\textbf{(L8) Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)'s condition is not operationally checkable as stated.} Regime~A's ordering-survival condition is $\rho < S$ with $S$ the attack's coordinate extremeness against the benign scale. Building the dose--response ladder (§\ref{sec:dose_response}) required us to \emph{measure} $S$ rather than stipulate it, and it is not a stable per-attack constant: on model-scaling it ranges $0.00$--$12.59$ across the 12 measured rounds that sampled an adversary (median $0.57$), on the pixel backdoor $0.61$--$1.63$ (median $0.92$). The $S{=}10$ of §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem} is an illustrative stipulation, not a measurement. Two consequences we state rather than work around. First, a practitioner cannot check $\rho < S$ from a single round: the quantity swings by orders of magnitude round to round, and on both committed attacks its median is below $1$, so Regime~A's condition fails even at the identity rung under this estimator. Second, we therefore built \emph{no} prediction in §\ref{sec:dose_response} on $S$, resting the ladder on directly measured disturbance of $d_2$'s statistic instead. The margin form of the theorem remains valid as a statement about $\rho$ and $S$; what is not established is that $S$ admits a stable estimator at the round granularity a deployment would have to check it at, and a usable Regime~A needs an $S$ estimator with characterized variance, which we do not provide.

\textbf{(L9) Measured disturbance is not a transferable predictor, and our dose instrument varies the wrong quantity for three of its four arms.} The pre-registered ladder (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}) refutes the pooled claim it was built to test ($\rho_s = +0.351$, $p = 0.091$) and refutes two of four per-arm shapes, with ASR \emph{falling} as the dose rises in three arms. Two distinct faults, both ours. First, the abscissa: at matched measured disturbance the real upstream transforms sit up to $+0.79$ ASR above the synthetic curve, and Krum's within-margin arm does not reproduce the very contrast (§\ref{sec:metric_swap}) we attributed to disturbance of its statistic, so the rate at which a transform flips a defense's decision is not a sufficient statistic for what the transform does---\emph{which} clients are reweighted matters, and $\rho$ does not measure that. We now measure the quantity that does, $\Delta_c$ (Appendix~\ref{app:delta_c}): the two anchors that miss are the two most extremely targeted ($-1.21$, $-0.72$ against the ladder's $-0.34$) at $\rho$ that is $3.7$ and $13\times$ smaller. That names the axis of the mismatch without supplying a replacement predictor---$\Delta_c$'s magnitude does not order suppression either. Second, the instrument: mean-1 normalization fixes the aggregate's scale but not any client's relative weight, so with the dose assigned independently of adversary status a large $\rho$ mostly dilutes whichever client carries the poison (measured: the ladder's $\Delta_c$ drifts monotonically negative with the dose, $0.000 \to -0.140 \to -0.240 \to -0.336$), and three arms end up measuring Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s attenuation regime rather than C2 disturbance. The adversary-targeted dose that separates the two, recorded as not run before the freeze (caveat (iii), Appendix~\ref{app:dose_caveats}), is run in §\ref{sec:targeted}---and in retrospect Mode~S is the \textbf{$\Delta_c$-pinned} instrument ($\Delta_c$ exactly $0$ at every rung, $\rho$ up to $54.6$) and Mode~A the \textbf{$\Delta_c$-swept} one ($-1.14$ to $+2.47$): with every adversary's weight pinned, Krum's statistic is disturbed in up to $80\%$ of rounds while the admission of adversarial input does not move and ASR stays inside the pre-specified equivalence margin---so \emph{which} clients are reweighted, not how often a decision flips, is indeed the operative variable, and the ladder confound is \emph{explained} (reputation's rise shrinks from $+0.762$ to $+0.178$ once the adversarial share is held fixed). That isolates C2's causal content within a single defense; it does not rescue the pooled positive direction ($R^2 = 0.239$ over $26$ cells, cross-arm ordering refuted by the replication).

\section{Conclusion}
\label{sec:conclusion}

\textbf{The result of this paper is a causal one, not a screening rule.} With the downstream defense, the attack, and standalone effectiveness all held fixed, and with the intervention applied \emph{directly} to the statistic that defense reads, changing that statistic can have little or no effect on suppression---and in the same cell the effect \textbf{reverses sign} once adversarial influence is unpinned and allowed to co-vary with it ($-0.272$ against $+0.098$). So positive per-client rescaling admits invariance-based guarantees (Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}) sufficient for preserving the \emph{downstream discriminative mechanism}, never for low ASR; and \emph{any} criterion gated on the outcome it predicts carries a \textbf{structural identification boundary} (Proposition~\ref{prop:identification}, with Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} its FL instance) that no amount of data removes. \textbf{The boundary is general where our empirical claims are not}, and what we offer as transferable is the \textbf{admissible design} it derives, which §\ref{sec:targeted} runs.

\paragraph{The three central empirical findings.} \emph{(i) The negative itself}: with the attenuation channel closed by construction, disturbing the statistic Krum reads in up to $80\%$ of rounds moves neither the adversarial mass admitted ($0.000$) nor ASR, and a pre-registered replication reproduces it in a different invariance class and on a second dataset and architecture (§\ref{sec:targeted}). \emph{(ii) The sign reversal}: on the \emph{identical} cell the outcome-gated ladder \emph{falls} $0.272$ ($p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0015$) where the instrument that pins adversarial influence \emph{rises} $+0.098$---a sign inversion under a change of \emph{intervention}, not of data, which is the identification boundary of Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} appearing as a measured effect. \emph{(iii) Heterogeneity closes the theorem's margin}: Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound is a function of the realized margin $m$ alone, and at Dirichlet $\alpha{=}0.1$ RFA's all-round mean margin collapses from $m{=}3.24$ to $m{=}1.01$ ($4.71 \to 2.06$ over payload-carrying rounds)---the limit at which the corollary certifies nothing---with two of three certified compositions crossing the threshold there (§\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E)). Here the invariance theory earns explanatory value in \emph{both} directions: it predicts a number that holds where heterogeneity is moderate ($12.6\%$ bound against a realized ${\le}1.8\%$) and its own quantity records where it stops holding.

Sequential defense composition cannot be reliably screened by asking whether an upstream transform preserves a downstream \emph{statistic} alone: of Definition~\ref{def:levels}'s five preservation levels the chain holds only down to (P3), and below that it breaks in \emph{both} directions. \textbf{The useful object to screen is not statistic invariance itself, but whether the upstream transform changes the adversarial contribution that survives the downstream defense---level (P4).} What the paper contributes is that mismatch, made formal and then measured: a hierarchy of preservation levels, an \textbf{identification boundary} showing that \emph{any} screening criterion whose eligibility condition is defined on the outcome it predicts creates the same confound whatever the domain, and the within-defense interventions that boundary licenses. \textbf{Mechanism-preserving screening}---checkable sufficient conditions, C1$\wedge$C2 with C3 retained as a margin diagnostic, for \emph{preservation of the downstream discriminative mechanism} and never for low ASR, which they do not imply---is what we build on top of that boundary and is the paper's \emph{application}, not its claim.

\paragraph{What is proved, what is empirically supported, and what is not established.} \textbf{Proved:} the invariance classes of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}; sufficient conditions for mechanism preservation on the weighting$\to$aggregator class (Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}, Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}); the identification boundary for outcome-gated screening (Proposition~\ref{prop:identification}, with Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} its FL instance); the recall bound on inherited-suppression screens (Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent}); and, formalizing the interventions, statistic-level non-identifiability (Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability}) and the independent controllability of $\rho$ and $\Delta_c$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:rho_deltac}). \textbf{The two boundary results are general}---they are statements about the shape of a screen, not about FL---so what carries beyond this paper is the design they license, not the screen. \textbf{Empirically supported:} that substantial disturbance of the downstream statistic need not produce a corresponding change in suppression when the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed, with adversarial admission the channel the evidence favours, \emph{on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S} and, by pre-registered replication, both on a downstream defense in a \emph{different} invariance class and on a \emph{second dataset and architecture} (§\ref{sec:targeted}); and that the screen behaves as a conservative prioritizer across 42 pairs. \textbf{Not established:} the positive predictive direction of the criterion---the pooled two-channel model is unconfirmed over $26$ cells ($R^2{=}0.239$) and the replication refutes its cross-arm admission ordering, so admission is a direction and not a quantitative predictor; any ability to discover emergent synergy; and generalization beyond star-topology FL, or beyond CIFAR-10 for anything but the one arm replicated on EMNIST-byclass.

\paragraph{What the screen is for.} It \textbf{trades discovery for reliability}: asking whether $d_2$'s suppression \emph{survives} $d_1$ certifies only \textbf{inherited} robustness, and \textbf{Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent} proves it cannot find compositions that suppress because \emph{neither} constituent does}---FG$\to$RFA ($0.093$) is exactly such a case and remains a false negative of our own rule, and the bound binds at the $1/5$ that gives the reported $40\%$ recall. That is a scope theorem rather than a defect discovered, and it is specific neither to C1 nor to federated learning. We also do not offer the screen's precision as validation: it rests on three positive predictions under the frozen labels---two under the tightened per-attack C1---in a badly imbalanced evaluation. What survives validation is a \textbf{conservative evaluation-prioritization rule for inherited robustness, not a predictor of emergent synergy}: its output answers \emph{which compositions are worth evaluating first}, never which to deploy. The criterion is partially formal---Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} proves C2$\wedge$C3 for the weighting$\to$aggregator class with explicit margins, so that class needs no calibration runs---and extensively \emph{scored}: $90.5\%$ on 42 two-way pairs (24 pre-registered and held out), $90\%$ on 10 pre-registered triples drawn from 210 candidates, and better than $99.99\%$ of random selections ($p < 10^{-4}$). A prospective suite of 20 pairs on three previously unused defenses, frozen in git before any run, scores 13/20 and 11/13 in scope, exposed the scope condition C0, and supplied no falsifiable prospective \emph{positive} test. A second pre-registered suite built to fix that did not supply one either: its own pre-freeze baselines showed that for this defense family the magnitude information an invariant mirror discards is what the original uses to suppress a magnitude attack, so \textbf{the confound re-materialized inside the experiment designed to remove it} ($8/8$ frozen labels, TP${}=0$). We name that obstacle \textbf{mechanism--effectiveness confounding} and show via Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} that our own conditions force it---a \textbf{testability boundary} we state in general form (§\ref{sec:testability}): any composition criterion whose screening conditions include a standalone-effectiveness gate on the same outcome metric it predicts admits only within-defense validation designs. Read as a derivation rather than an obstacle, the boundary specifies the experiment---hold $d_2$ and the attack fixed so the C1 input is numerically identical on both sides, and vary only measured disturbance of $d_2$'s statistic. Post-hoc it already separates preserved from destroyed suppression ($\Delta \in [+0.001,+0.190]$ vs.\ $[+0.718,+0.878]$ at $n{=}5$), Krum under model-scaling moving from $0.062$ to $0.779$ as its selection goes from never disturbed to disturbed in $6/9$ rounds (Welch $p = 0.012$); prospectively, §\ref{sec:dose_response} runs it as a pre-registered dose--response ladder over $\rho$ up to $54.6$ with independently measured disturbance---and \textbf{that ladder largely refutes us}: the pooled prediction fails ($\rho_s = +0.351$, $p = 0.091$), two of four frozen shapes are refuted, ASR falls with dose in three arms, and at matched measured disturbance the real transforms sit up to $0.79$ above the synthetic curve. One arm confirms (reputation $0.017 \to 0.779$, $p = 1.7\times10^{-6}$, accuracy $0.78 \to 0.70$), covering the destruction half alone---and the targeted suite later shows most of that rise was attenuation rather than statistic disturbance (\S\ref{sec:targeted}). So measured disturbance of a defense's statistic is \emph{not} a transferable predictor of whether composition preserves suppression, and \emph{which} clients an upstream transform reweights---not how often it flips a decision---is where the mechanism must be looked for (L9). A targeted intervention does exactly that (§\ref{sec:targeted}): with the attenuation channel closed by construction, Krum's statistic is disturbed in up to $80\%$ of rounds while its \emph{admission} of adversarial input and its ASR do not move, showing that substantial disturbance of the statistic need not produce a corresponding change in suppression and that admission is the channel the evidence points to \emph{on that adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S}---the controlled within-defense test of C2 our cross-defense suites could not supply. A pre-registered replication on a downstream defense in a \emph{different} invariance class, on an arm the two readings order oppositely, reproduces that negative and \textbf{refutes the positive ordering}, leaving the positive direction unvalidated as a screen (pooled over $26$ cells, $R^2 = 0.239$). What the screen bounds throughout is \textbf{inherited} robustness under a \emph{committed} attack; a screening condition stated against an \emph{adaptive} adversary is the sharpest open direction the framework leaves. Among evaluated compositions the criterion identifies an accuracy--security Pareto frontier: FG$\to$CM ($0.107$ / $0.68$) as the best candidate for further evaluation, FG$\to$RFA ($0.093$ / $0.50$) as the lowest-observed-ASR extreme though not separably so, and Rep$\to$CM ($0.423$ / $0.76$) for near-baseline accuracy at moderate security (Table~\ref{tab:pareto}).

Deterministic composition outperforms temporal mixing (per-round defense switching) on every tested menu and adversary policy in the persistent-backdoor regime. The mechanism: once a trigger embeds ($\gamma \approx 0.66$), intermittent suppression cannot clear it, making mixes converge to their worst constituent's ASR. Committed strategies are near-optimal (largest switching gain $+0.044$, below bound $0.048$).

The regime boundary's quantitative location is architecture-conditional (ResNet18 shifts thresholds by ${\sim}10\times$); extrapolation to cross-device FL ($N{\geq}10^4$, $f{\leq}0.01$) requires additional experiments. The qualitative predictions---PASS/FAIL classification, composition dominance, committed-strategy near-optimality---generalize across architecture (CNN$\to$ResNet18), scale ($N{=}10 \to 100$), adversarial fraction ($f \in [0.05, 0.6]$), and attack family (model\_scaling/pixel$\to$DBA). \textbf{Heterogeneity is the exception and we state it here rather than only in the sweep}: at $\alpha{=}0.1$ two of the three certified compositions cross the threshold, and the failure appears in the theorem's own quantity (RFA's separation margin collapsing to $m \approx 1$; §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E)).

\paragraph{What to do instead: six steps for any composition claim.} This is the admissible design of §\ref{sec:testability} in operational form, and it is the practical contribution rather than the criterion. \textbf{(1)} Name the downstream mechanism and its invariance class. \textbf{(2)} State \emph{which} preservation level you are claiming, (P1)--(P5) of Definition~\ref{def:levels}; a claim that does not name its level is not checkable. \textbf{(3)} Check that the upstream transform lies in the class your argument actually covers---C0 here (Remark~\ref{rem:c0}); one that alters participation, carries cross-round state, or mixes clients is covered by no invariance argument we or anyone else has. \textbf{(4)} Hold $d_2$, the attack, and therefore the gate's input fixed, and \textbf{do not select candidates on the outcome}---this is the cell Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} shows is otherwise empty. \textbf{(5)} Intervene on the upstream transform and \textbf{measure the channel}---aggregate displacement, decision, admission, influence---rather than the outcome-conditional contrast. \textbf{(6)} Report the level at which the chain broke. Steps (4)--(5) are exactly what Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} licenses, and following them turned our own $-0.272$ into $+0.098$ on an identical cell. \textbf{One boundary the recipe does not cross}: under \emph{one} whitebox composition-aware \emph{adaptive} stress test FG$\to$RFA rises to $\mathrm{ASR} = 0.167 \pm 0.069$, $3.3\times$ its seed-matched committed $0.051$, so \textbf{an inherited-robustness guarantee is not adaptive robustness} (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}). That is one adaptive test on one composition and we do not generalize it; a screening condition stated against an adaptive adversary is the sharpest thing the framework lacks.

\paragraph{The negative is the result.} Of Definition~\ref{def:levels}'s five preservation levels the chain holds only down to (P3); below that it breaks in \emph{both} directions, each with a measured witness: an exactly-invariant defense whose selection never moves while its ASR falls $0.173$ by its first gate-passing rung, and a defense whose selection flips in up to $80\%$ of rounds with its ASR stays inside the pre-specified equivalence margin ($|\Delta|{=}0.026 < 0.15$). Our own statistic-level hypothesis is refuted pooled, and the admission reading that replaced it survives as a \emph{direction}, not a predictor: the arm we pre-registered to order it refuted that ordering. \textbf{So the object worth screening is not whether an upstream transform preserves a downstream statistic. It is level (P4), the adversarial contribution that survives the downstream defense.} Why we cannot simply screen for it is worth stating precisely, because it is the question this paper leaves open. Mode~S \emph{does} measure (P4) directly---that is what makes it an identification instrument---but it computes the adversarial share by reading adversary identity, which no deployed defense has. So the experiments locate the causal channel and \textbf{do not} constitute a deployable (P4) screen. \textbf{The open problem is whether (P4) can be estimated without knowing which clients are malicious}: whether some observable functional of the transformed updates bounds the adversarial mass a downstream aggregator admits. A screen at (P4) would inherit this paper's negative result as its justification; a proof that no such estimator exists would be a stronger negative than the one we report.

\section{Dose--response design: the full pre-registered protocol}
\label{app:dose_protocol}

Four properties make this a dial on the theory rather than an arbitrary perturbation. (i) It is exactly Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s form $T(u_i)=c_iu_i$ with $c_i>0$, so every rung lies inside the theory's scope. (ii) \textbf{The dose is $\rho$}: $\max_j c_j/\min_j c_j = e^{2\kappa}$ exactly, the same upstream weight ratio Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} and both mechanism-preserving regimes are stated in---verified by reading $\rho$ back off the \emph{transformed update norms} on live rounds, where realized $\rho$ matches nominal to three decimals at every rung and $\mathrm{mean}(c)$ stays in $[0.99999, 1.00002]$. (iii) $\kappa{=}0$ gives $c \equiv 1$: the implementation short-circuits, so the identity rung is bit-identical to $d_2$ standalone by construction rather than by rounding. (iv) Normalizing the \emph{mean} to 1 holds the aggregate's scale fixed, separating the quantity under test (disturbance of $d_2$'s \emph{statistic}, i.e.\ C2) from the magnitude-contraction channel of Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} that costs accuracy; without it the ladder would confound C2 with attenuation. The permutation is keyed on $(\text{seed},\text{round})$ and \textbf{not} on client identity: assigning by id would place the adversaries at a fixed end of the ladder and systematically attenuate them, manufacturing a success, whereas this keying gives every arm at a given seed and round the \emph{identical} coefficient vector, so the arms differ in $d_2$ and nothing else. \textbf{$\texttt{dose\_kappa}\langle\kappa\rangle$ is an instrument, not a defense}, and is never evaluated or recommended as one.

\textbf{Frozen grid and arms.} $\kappa \in \{0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0\}$, i.e.\ $\rho \in \{1.00, 2.72, 7.39, 54.60\}$: four rungs spanning a ${\approx}55\times$ ratio. Arms are selected by the paper's own power rule (§\ref{sec:metric_swap}), fixed before the freeze: the ladder is informative only where standalone $d_2$ genuinely suppresses the attack (ASR $<0.5$ at clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$), since a baseline already above threshold has no suppression left to lose and would give a flat curve by ceiling effect. Applying that rule to the measured baselines leaves exactly four $(d_2, \text{attack})$ cells, and they happen to cover three invariance classes of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}). The $\kappa{=}0$ rung is run \emph{fresh} at all five seeds for every arm rather than reused from the published baselines, so every rung of every curve comes from one code path at one seed set; the published baselines then serve as an independent per-seed cross-check of that rung (tolerance $10^{-6}$) instead of as data in it.

\textbf{The abscissa is measured, not nominal.} What matters to C2 is not $\rho$ but what the rescaling does to the specific statistic $d_2$ reads, so we measure that separately and \emph{before} freezing any prediction, pushing live FL updates through the shipped transform and recording each arm's decision at every $\kappa$ on the \emph{same} raw updates (\texttt{measure\_dose\_disturbance.py}, \texttt{results/dose\_disturbance.json}; 5 seeds $\times$ 3 rounds per attack). No ASR is computed there, which is why it can legitimately precede the freeze. It establishes three things before any outcome exists (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}): \emph{there is a dose}---disturbance is exactly $0$ at the identity rung and rises monotonically with $\kappa$ in every non-invariant arm; \emph{the class-(a) invariance claim holds in the shipped code}---\texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is unchanged in $\mathbf{0}$ of $120$ cells at $\rho$ up to $54.6$, its cosine \emph{scores} moving by at most $1.1\times10^{-4}$ relative, the float32 floor for a ${\approx}1.1$M-dimensional vector, which we record rather than assert on; and \emph{class (b) is graded rather than binary}, at roughly half the class-(c) disturbance ($0.487$ vs.\ $0.867$/$0.933$ at $\kappa{=}2.0$), which is what arm 4's prediction turns on.

\textbf{Frozen predictions.} The \emph{primary} test is pooled: across all 16 $(\text{arm},\kappa)$ cells, the rise in ASR over that arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung is predicted to be positively rank-correlated with the measured disturbance rate (Spearman, one-sided, $\alpha = 0.05$). This is the dose--response claim the design exists for, and its predictor is a \emph{measured property of the statistic}---not the identity of the defense and not its standalone effectiveness, both held fixed within every arm. The \emph{secondary} predictions are the per-arm shapes of Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}, tested by a one-sided Jonckheere--Terpstra trend test across the four ordered rungs at $n{=}5$. For arm 3, ``flat'' is scored as an \emph{equivalence} claim rather than a failed rejection: confirmed if $|\overline{\mathrm{ASR}}(\kappa{=}2.0) - \overline{\mathrm{ASR}}(\kappa{=}0)| < 0.15$ \emph{and} every rung stays below $0.5$. That margin is fixed and justified in advance: it is under a quarter of the $+0.629$ within-defense gap for Krum on record at the freeze (the post-hoc top-up later put that gap at $+0.716$ at $n{=}5$, which only makes the margin more conservative---it is not revised), and $0.300 + 0.15 = 0.450 < 0.5$, so ``within the margin'' and ``still suppressing'' coincide by design for this arm. Arm 4's class-(b) signature is the \emph{shallower slope}, so its criterion includes its $\kappa{=}2.0$ rise being smaller than both class-(c) arms'.


\section{Out-of-sample PASS validation: per-cell detail}
\label{app:oos_detail}

\textbf{(A) DBA attack transfer.} The Distributed Backdoor Attack~\citep{xie2019dba}---a categorically different attack family using spatially-distributed sub-triggers at four image corners---is not in the development menu ($f{=}0.4$ required for viability at $K{=}5$). We test three PASS-predicted compositions (fg$\to$rfa, fg$\to$cm, rep$\to$cm) and three FAIL controls (nc$\to$rep, rfa$\to$rep, fg$\to$tm) under DBA at $f{=}0.4$, $N{=}10$, $n{=}5$ seeds. Results: all three PASS compositions robustly suppress DBA (fg$\to$rfa: $0.109 \pm 0.065$; fg$\to$cm: $0.050 \pm 0.027$; rep$\to$cm: $0.041 \pm 0.014$). The FAIL controls show weaker discrimination than under model\_scaling/pixel: nc$\to$rep $0.297 \pm 0.224$, rfa$\to$rep $0.297 \pm 0.159$, fg$\to$tm $0.049 \pm 0.031$. This reflects DBA's inherent variability at $f{=}0.4$/$K{=}5$ (sanity baseline vs.\ fedavg: $0.236 \pm 0.190$, bimodal across seeds). Nevertheless, the distributions separate at the tails: max per-seed ASR across all PASS pairs $= 0.185$, while FAIL pairs reach $0.712$ (Appendix~\ref{app:oos_pass}).

\emph{Powered re-test at $f{=}0.6$.} To address the power limitation (sanity baseline $0.236$, never clearing $0.5$, making FAIL discrimination unavailable), we re-ran all six pairs under DBA at $f{=}0.6$ ($K{\cdot}f{=}3.0$; $n{=}5$ seeds). PASS predictions confirmed: fg$\to$rfa $0.125{\pm}0.050$, fg$\to$cm $0.045{\pm}0.021$, rep$\to$cm $0.043{\pm}0.015$. FAIL controls: nc$\to$rep $0.378{\pm}0.185$, rfa$\to$rep $0.384{\pm}0.217$, fg$\to$tm $0.068{\pm}0.018$. Sanity baseline (DBA vs.\ fedavg, no defense composition): $0.386{\pm}0.192$---still below $0.5$ due to DBA's distributed co-sampling bottleneck (Appendix~\ref{app:dba_f06}). FAIL discrimination therefore remains unavailable even at $f{=}0.6$: DBA does not reliably embed at this $N$/$K$ regardless of defense. The symmetric verdict: criterion PASS predictions are confirmed robustly at both adversarial fractions; FAIL-side falsifiability requires a DBA variant that embeds reliably before those controls are meaningful.

\textbf{(B) Scale transfer ($N{=}100$, ResNet18).} We test fg$\to$rfa and fg$\to$cm at $N{=}100$, $K{=}10$, $f{=}0.05$, ResNet18---a $10\times$ client-pool expansion with a different architecture---having pre-registered both as PASS under committed\_scaling and committed\_pixel. At $n{=}5$ seeds: fg$\to$rfa max ASR $= 0.069 \pm 0.029$, fg$\to$cm $= 0.152 \pm 0.118$. \textbf{All four conditions confirmed PASS.} We flag that these runs reach only ${\approx}0.29$ clean accuracy at 50 rounds---ResNet18 is far from converged at this budget---so they establish that \emph{suppression transfers} across scale and architecture, not that the composition is deployable at $N{=}100$ as configured. The single-defense comparison sharpens the picture: RFA alone reaches committed\_scaling ASR $0.410 \pm 0.386$ (bimodal; some seeds embed, others do not) against fg$\to$rfa's $0.069 \pm 0.031$, a $6\times$ improvement at $N{=}100$. Two FAIL-predicted pairs at the same scale (nc$\to$rep C2-FAIL, fg$\to$tm C3-FAIL; $n{=}5$) came out at $0.279$ and $0.167$---below $0.5$, mirroring the DBA power limitation: at $f{=}0.05$ committed attacks do not reliably embed regardless of defense, so FAIL-side discrimination is unavailable at this scale/fraction combination. The positive result is the substantive finding.

\textbf{(C) Independent survivor (fg$\to$cm).} We pre-registered that fg$\to$cm---selected \emph{solely} by criterion PASS status, \emph{independent of the 189-config payoff-matrix scan} that selected rep+tm---dominates temporal mixing of fg/cm. This is independence from the survivor scan, not full data-independence: fg$\to$cm also appears in the development set (Table~\ref{tab:composability}, $0.107$). Two conditions ($n{=}5$): composed (always fg$\to$cm) vs.\ mixed (50/50 random per round). We anchor the headline on the \textbf{committed-pixel} policy, where both conditions retain a usable model (clean accuracy $0.66$ composed, $0.77$ mixed): composed ASR $= 0.099 \pm 0.020$ vs.\ mixed $= 0.679 \pm 0.159$, a $6.9\times$ suppression. On committed\_scaling the mixed model is \emph{destroyed} (clean accuracy $0.11 \approx$ CIFAR-10 chance), so its ASR is not a meaningful backdoor comparison---no operator would deploy an accuracy-collapsing policy---and we exclude it from the headline. The clean committed-pixel comparison alone establishes composition dominance on a scan-independent menu.

\section{Dose--response caveats recorded before the freeze}
\label{app:dose_caveats}

\textbf{Caveats recorded in advance.} (i) Arms 1--2 are on model-scaling and arms 3--4 on the pixel backdoor. This is not a free choice: on model-scaling \emph{no} invariant $d_2$ suppresses at usable accuracy---that is the finding of §\ref{sec:metric_swap} (\texttt{cos\_krum} $0.078$ at $0.146$ accuracy)---which is also why \texttt{cos\_krum}/scaling is not an arm. ``Same attack'' is required \emph{within} each ladder, which is where the causal claim lives; across arms we compare curve \emph{shapes and slopes}, never levels. (ii) Arm 4 has thin headroom: \texttt{coord\_median}/pixel sits only $0.057$ below threshold, so the binary above/below-$0.5$ reading is weak evidence either way and we report the continuous curve with intervals. (iii) The dose is uncorrelated with adversary status by construction, but it is not adversary-\emph{adversarial}: a dose assignment that deliberately up-weights adversaries would be a strictly harder test and is not run here. (iv) A synthetic upstream transform is not a real defense, so we test ecological validity by overlaying the two \emph{real} upstream transforms already measured on these same cells (NormClip and RFA, §\ref{sec:metric_swap}) as anchor points at their own measured disturbance; if they fall far off the synthetic curve at matched disturbance, that is a finding about the instrument and is reported as one. (v) Disturbance is measured on FedAvg trajectories on one shared set of raw updates per round---which is what makes it a clean abscissa, but means the rates in Table~\ref{tab:dose_design} are not re-measured along each arm's own 50-round trajectory.

 & $2.0$ ($54.6$) & $0.000$ & $0.086$ $[-0.100, 0.271]$ & $0.220^{\dagger}$ & $0.083$, $0.000$, $0.000$, $0.345$, $0.000$ \\

 & $0$ ($1.00$) & $0.000$ & $0.443$ $[0.263, 0.622]$ & $0.767$ & $0.395$, $0.287$, $0.449$, $0.677$, $0.405$ \\
 & $0.5$ ($2.72$) & $0.251$ & $0.428$ $[0.241, 0.615]$ & $0.764$ & $0.454$, $0.215$, $0.430$, $0.638$, $0.402$ \\

\textbf{The primary test, and one post-hoc sensitivity we report but do not lean on.} Pooled over all 16 cells, Spearman $\rho_s = +0.351$, one-sided $p = 0.091$ (the frozen test; refuted). Dropping the four $\kappa{=}0$ cells---which sit at $(0, 0)$ by construction rather than by measurement---raises this to $\rho_s = +0.780$, $p = 0.001$ over 12 cells. We were not entitled to that analysis in advance and it does not change the verdict, and its sign is in any case driven by the \emph{invariant} arm's ASR falling at zero disturbance being paired with the reputation arm's rising at high disturbance, not by the fragility pattern the framework claims: arms 1 and 4 contradict it. Against the \emph{nominal} dose $\kappa$ (equivalently $\log \rho$) instead of measured disturbance, the pooled correlation is $\rho_s = -0.354$. Neither predictor works pooled.

\textbf{Per-arm trend tests.} Jonckheere--Terpstra across the four ordered rungs $\times$ 5 seeds, one-sided for increasing trend (normal approximation; a 20{,}000-draw permutation p-value with a fixed seed is reported alongside by the analysis script and agrees to the third decimal in every arm): arm 1 $J = 70.0$, $z = -0.34$, $p_{\uparrow} = 0.63$; arm 2 $J = 144.0$, $z = +4.64$, $p_{\uparrow} = 1.7\times10^{-6}$; arm 3 $J = 29.0$, $z = -3.10$, $p_{\uparrow} = 0.999$ ($p_{\downarrow} = 9.8\times10^{-4}$; dropping the gate-failing $\kappa{=}2.0$ rung leaves $z = -2.06$, $p_{\downarrow} = 0.020$); arm 4 $J = 31.0$, $z = -2.96$, $p_{\uparrow} = 0.998$ ($p_{\downarrow} = 1.5\times10^{-3}$). No frozen rule tests a decreasing trend, so those $p_{\downarrow}$ values are descriptive.

\textbf{Clean accuracy against dose (descriptive, no frozen rule).} Arm 1 $0.565 \to 0.652 \to 0.682 \to 0.619$ ($z = +1.35$); arm 2 $0.777 \to 0.779 \to 0.765 \to 0.701$ ($z = -3.30$); arm 3 $0.607 \to 0.569 \to 0.466 \to 0.220$ ($z = -3.50$); arm 4 $0.767 \to 0.764 \to 0.749 \to 0.690$ ($z = -4.11$). This is what separates a suppression effect from a contraction artifact: arm 4 loses $0.077$ of accuracy while its ASR more than halves, whereas arm 3 loses $0.39$.

\textbf{Anchor residuals (pre-registered caveat (iv)).} Disturbance rates from \texttt{cos\_invariance\_check.json}, ASRs recomputed from \texttt{metric\_swap/} at the frozen $n{=}3$ and from \texttt{metric\_swap\_topup/} at $n{=}5$; the residual is against the synthetic rung nearest in measured disturbance. NormClip$\to$Krum $0/9 = 0.000$, ASR $0.062 \to 0.062$, residual $+0.001$; RFA$\to$Krum $6/9 = 0.667$, $0.691 \to 0.779$, residual $\mathbf{+0.767}$; NormClip$\to$Reputation $3/9 = 0.333$, $0.792 \to 0.806$, residual $\mathbf{+0.789}$; RFA$\to$Reputation $9/9 = 1.000$, $0.882 \to 0.895$, residual $+0.116$; NormClip$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} $0/9$, $0.300 \to 0.490$, residual $+0.000$; RFA$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} $0/9$, $0.273 \to 0.447$, residual $-0.044$. Realized upstream $\rho$ on the same rounds: NormClip mean $3.61$ (max $4.90$), RFA mean $11.93$ (max $17.38$).

\section{Notation}
\label{app:notation}

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
symbol & meaning \\
\midrule
$c_i$ & upstream coefficient applied to client $i$'s update, $T(u_i) = c_iu_i$, $c_i>0$ \\
$A$ & the set of adversarial participants in a round \\
$\rho$ & statistic-level dispersion, $\max_i c_i/\min_i c_i$: \emph{how far apart} the coefficients are \\
$\Delta_c$ & adversary-correlated dispersion, $\mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A]-\mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$: \emph{who} receives them \\
$r$ & adversary-to-benign weight ratio, $w_a/\max_b w_b$; the Mode-A dial, $r = e^{\nu}$ \\
$S$ & the attack's coordinate extremeness (Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)) \\
$m$ & RFA separation margin, $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/2(R'_B{+}\delta')$ \\
$\Lambda_a$ & aggregate adversarial influence: adversarial share of the Weiszfeld mass \\
$\kappa$ & the Mode-S / ladder dispersion dial, $\rho = e^{2\kappa}$ \\
$\gamma$ & attack \emph{persistence}, $\mathrm{ASR}(t{+}1)/\mathrm{ASR}(t)$ after a clean round ($\approx 0.66$); never a weight \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\section{Decomposing the emitted displacement: re-selection against rescaling}
\label{app:decomposition}

\noindent The $\Delta$~agg.\ column of Table~\ref{tab:channels} mixes two things the causal claim of §\ref{sec:targeted} needs apart. For a selector, $\mathrm{agg}(U) = u_{s_0}$ and $\mathrm{agg}(T(U)) = c_{s_1} u_{s_1}$, with $s_0$ the client selected on the raw stack and $s_1$ the client selected on the transformed one, so exactly
\[ c_{s_1}u_{s_1} - u_{s_0} \;=\; \underbrace{(u_{s_1} - u_{s_0})}_{\text{re-selection}} \;+\; \underbrace{(c_{s_1}-1)\,u_{s_1}}_{\text{rescaling}}, \]
both components reported relative to $\|u_{s_0}\|$. The re-selection component is what a magnitude-controlled dose would still move---it takes both updates \emph{untransformed}---while the rescaling component is what such a control closes. They are \textbf{not orthogonal}: the measured angle runs from $\cos{=}-0.99$ to $-0.43$, mean ${\approx}-0.65$, so their norms need not sum to the total, and the table prints the angle rather than leaving that discrepancy unexplained. What is asserted instead is the exact vector identity above: worst residual $2.66\mathrm{e}{-}07$ over all $240$ (arm, family, rung, seed, round) cells, float32 noise. \textbf{This decomposes the published number, not a second similar one}: every recomputed total agrees with the corresponding $\Delta$~agg.\ value in \texttt{results/admission\_measurement.json} to $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$, on the same $5$ seeds $\times$ $3$ live rounds, the same rungs and the same configuration as that measurement. \textbf{Selectors only}---a weighted averager and a coordinate-wise order statistic emit no single selected client, so ``the selected update'' and hence this split are undefined for them, and \texttt{coord\_median} and \texttt{reputation} do not appear. \texttt{experiments/measure\_displacement\_decomposition.py} trains no model per rung, computes no ASR, writes a new file (\texttt{results/displacement\_decomposition.json}) and leaves every frozen artifact byte-identical.

\begin{center}\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{llrrrrrr}
\toprule
Design & Selector & $\kappa$ & total & re-selection & rescaling & $\cos$ & sel.\ chg. \\
\midrule
Confounded ladder & \texttt{krum} & $0$ & $0.000$ & $\mathbf{0.000}$ & $0.000$ & --- & $0.000$ \\
 &  & $0.5$ & $0.637$ & $\mathbf{0.685}$ & $0.333$ & $-0.65$ & $0.600$ \\
 &  & $1$ & $0.731$ & $\mathbf{0.851}$ & $0.685$ & $-0.66$ & $0.733$ \\

\midrule

 &  & $2$ & $0.834$ & $\mathbf{0.000}$ & $0.834$ & --- & $0.000$ \\
\midrule
Instrument (Mode~S) & \texttt{cos\_krum} & $0$ & $0.000$ & $\mathbf{0.000}$ & $0.000$ & --- & $0.000$ \\
 &  & $0.5$ & $0.395$ & $\mathbf{0.000}$ & $0.395$ & --- & $0.000$ \\

\end{center}

\noindent \textbf{What it says, and what it does not.} Mode~S into Krum at $\kappa{=}2$: the published $0.892$ is $0.986$ of re-selection and $0.973$ of rescaling, so the dose moves \emph{which} benign client is aggregated and \emph{how large} it arrives, in comparable measure---the instrument is not quietly a magnitude-only perturbation, nor quietly a decision-only one. Of the $11/15$ rounds whose selection changes there, \textbf{none} lands on an adversary; that is the quantitative form of the claim that Krum's decision changes are benign-to-benign, and the reason admitted adversarial mass stays at exactly $0.000$ while the decision channel moves by $0.733$. \texttt{cos\_krum} is the informative contrast: its statistic is exactly invariant to positive rescaling, so $0/15$ selections change at every rung of \emph{both} designs and its entire displacement---$1.297$ under the instrument, $0.834$ under the confounded ladder---is rescaling. Its fall therefore cannot travel through the decision channel at all, which corroborates §\ref{sec:targeted}'s attribution of that fall to the magnitude channel from a quantity measured independently of any ASR. \textbf{This is a decomposition of a displacement, not of an ASR effect}: it says what the dose moves in the update entering training, makes no claim about suppression, and is an analysis of runs already reported rather than a new experiment.

\section{Adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c$, measured}
\label{app:delta_c}

$\Delta_c := \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ for every per-client transform in the paper, over the same $5$ seeds $\times$ $3$ rounds and, within a round, the \emph{same} raw update stack for every transform, so the rows are comparable by construction (\texttt{measure\_coefficient\_targeting.py}, \texttt{results/coefficient\_targeting.json}). \textbf{No ASR is computed and no model is trained per transform}, which is what lets this be added without rescoring anything; the figures are \textbf{post-hoc and exploratory} and no frozen prediction depends on them. Coefficients are read back from transformed norms, $c_i = \|T(u_i)\|/\|u_i\|$, so the table measures the shipped code path rather than the builder's description of it. As a provenance check the synthetic families' adversarial coefficient shares reproduce \texttt{results/admission\_measurement.json} to $0.0$ absolute on all 13 cells, so the rows of Table~\ref{tab:delta_c} may be read on the same axis as the Mode-S and Mode-A figures of §\ref{sec:targeted}.

Means over non-degenerate rounds; the synthetic rows are attack-pooled since their coefficients do not depend on the attack.}
